BotDesigner is a no-code prompt design tool that the authors built so they could watch how non-experts intuitively approach prompt design. The participants are not a test set, and the passages do not describe comparing their prompts against the probe.
  • [From the paper] What the probe is for: The authors say a hands-on tool gives deeper insight than interviews about hypothetical scenarios, and that an open-ended design lets them observe participants' intuitive behaviors and thought processes. The probe is therefore a way to elicit and study behaviour, and the study's aim is to understand how non-experts approach designing robust prompts and where they struggle.
  • [Explanation] Why the baseline/test-set analogy does not fit: A baseline model and a test set are used to measure performance on a fixed task. Here, the probe sets up a task in which people's behaviour is observed and interpreted qualitatively, with the authors' findings about over-generalization and human-human expectations emerging from that observation. The passages describe no benchmark or comparison of prompts against the probe.
  • [For your research] What this suggests for your user study: If you use a probe-like tool, it may be better to treat it as an instrument that shapes and reveals users' prompting behaviour, not as a reference system. The paper also notes that the sample is small, skewed toward STEM professionals and students, and not representative, so you would need to think about how your own participant sampling and any comparisons would be designed. You would need to decide yourself whether a probe fits your research questions.
Learner message
Thanks, that makes sense about the probe. Now I want to check how strong the evidence is. How many participants were in the study, and how did they code or analyse the data? If the sample is small, can I still trust the findings about over-generalization?
Move the simulated learner was given
Ask whether the evidence for these findings is strong, e.g. sample size.
PaperLens reply (expand a source to see the cited text; highlighted = evidence quote that occurs in it)
Short answer
The paper reports ten participants, analysed qualitatively from think-aloud videos through exploratory analysis and affinity diagramming by two authors. The sample was chosen to find common problems rather than to estimate how often they occur, so the over-generalization finding is best read as a pattern seen in this group. The passages I retrieved do not report inter-coder agreement, so reliability cannot be confirmed from them.
d606a330-p007-c03 · PDF p. 7 · Original passage
Participants. All participants ( = 10) had little to no experience with prompt design; none had substantial prior experience 2Regression tests, in the context of software engineering and in the sense we intend here, are tests that verify that any new changes to a system do not reintroduce previously-resolved errors (called regressions). working with LLMs. We recruited participants with varying levels of programming and chatbot design experience. Recall that our focus here is understanding end-users in the domain of prompt design; experience in chatbot design was thus not considered grounds for exclusion. Our sample size was chosen in line with prior work around formative testing for usability [19, 43]: our goal is to explore the frst, “easy-to-fnd” problems users encounter when engaged in our task, and our experience with a pilot user suggested that problem discoverability was likely to be high. Our participant pool, skewed towards professionals and graduate students in STEM-related felds, is not representative of the population at large.
d606a330-p009-c00 · PDF p. 9 · Original passage
Why Johnny Can’t Prompt CHI ’23, April 23–28, 2023, Hamburg, Germany After this initial phase, again in the interest of observing subsequent behavior, we would ofer suggestions for prompts to try that were in line with the research around prompt design. Examples of this advice include “try reframing that ‘do not’ statement into a positive statement, say what it is you want the bot to say, not what you don’t want it to say” or “have you considered copying some of the Carla-Lizzie transcript directly into the preamble?” We did not intentionally vary this advice across participants; however, due to the unpredictable nature of which errors participants chose to focus on, not all participants received the same advice or advice in the same order, a limitation of our approach. Analysis & Evaluation. We asked participants to think aloud, then engaged in exploratory data analysis, transcribing all videos and observing what prompt design approaches participants attempted, and noting when participants appeared to struggle and where interviewers intervened.
d606a330-p009-c01 · PDF p. 9 · Original passage
Two of the authors then compared these approaches across participants and categorized their struggles using afnity diagrams [34] and by creating a service blueprint [5] that documents the specifc approaches individual participants attempt. We chose these methods from HCI and the feld of service design because our focus is on discovering opportunities created by a new technology, rather than understanding how an existing set of users engages in established work practices—a context in which a grounded theory approach would be more conventional. 4 USER STUDY FINDINGS We found the following: (1) Using BotDesigner, all participants were able to engage in ad hoc opportunistic iteration on prompts, with local testing of prompt changes. Two out of our ten participants required assistance in order to begin this process, but could then iterate on their own. (2) Participants’ struggles with generating prompts, evaluating prompt efectiveness, and explaining prompt efects, primarily stemmed from (a) over-generalization from single observations of success and failure of prompt changes; and (b) models of system behavior rooted in human-human interactions.
From the paper
What the paper reports about sample and analysis
The paper states that all participants (N = 10) had little to no prior prompt design experience, and that the sample size followed prior formative usability testing work. Data came from think-aloud sessions that were transcribed and explored, and two authors categorized struggles using affinity diagrams and a service blueprint. The authors say their approach was not grounded theory.

Limitation / uncertainty
What the sample can and cannot support
The authors explicitly frame the study as exploring the first, easy-to-find problems, so the sample supports identifying recurring behaviours, not estimating their prevalence or their effect in a wider population. The authors also say the pool skews towards STEM professionals and graduate students and is not representative. The retrieved passages do not describe inter-coder agreement, codebook development or saturation checks, so the reliability of the categorization is unverified.

For your research
Implications for your user study
If your goal is to discover over-generalization patterns among non-experts, a qualitative design similar to this one may suit your question, but you would need to decide the sample size based on that aim. If you want claims about how common these patterns are, you would need a larger or more diverse sample, a clearly documented coding procedure, and reported inter-rater reliability. The final choice of method is yours to make.

C05

Paper: MDA: A Formal Approach to Game Design and Game Research Robin Hunicke, Marc LeBlanc, Robert Zubek
Reading goal: Get a general overview of this paper
Research context: (not provided)
Language setting: 中文（繁體）

Turn 1

For A · previous PaperLens reply
(first message of the conversation)
Learner message
這篇論文的作者想讓遊戲設計師拿這個框架來做什麼？是要幫忙設計遊戲，還是只是用來分析已經做好的遊戲？
Move the simulated learner was given
Ask what the authors want designers to do with this framework.
PaperLens reply (expand a source to see the cited text; highlighted = evidence quote that occurs in it)
Short answer
論文的立場是兩者都包含：MDA 主要是一個正式方法，既用來理解和拆解遊戲，也用來做迭代式的設計與調校。作者特別提到它能幫助設計師、研究者與評論者進行這種轉換。
f024c53f-p001-c02 · PDF p. 1 · Original passage
In this paper we present the MDA framework (standing for Mechanics, Dynamics, and Aesthetics), developed and taught as part of the Game Design and Tuning Workshop at the Game Developers Conference, San Jose 2001-2004 [LeBlanc, 2004a]. MDA is a formal approach to understanding games – one which attempts to bridge the gap between game design and development, game criticism, and technical game research. We believe this methodology will clarify and strengthen the iterative processes of developers, scholars and researchers alike, making it easier for all parties to decompose, study and design a broad class of game designs and game artifacts. Towards a Comprehensive Framework Game design and authorship happen at many levels, and the fields of games research and development involve people from diverse creative and scholarly backgrounds. While it’s often necessary to focus on one area, everyone, regardless of discipline, will at some point need to consider issues outside that area: base mechanisms of game systems, the overarching design goals, or the desired experiential results of gameplay.
f024c53f-p001-c03 · PDF p. 1 · Original passage
AI coders and researchers are no exception. Seemingly inconsequential decisions about data, representation, algorithms, tools, vocabulary and methodology will trickle upward, shaping the final gameplay. Similarly, all desired user experience must bottom out, somewhere, in code. As games continue to generate increasingly complex agent, object and system behavior, AI and game design merge. Systematic coherence comes when conflicting constraints are satisfied, and each of the game’s parts can relate to each other as a whole. D ecomposing, understanding and creating this coherence requires travel between all levels of abstraction – fluent motion from systems and code, to content and play experience, and back. We propose the MDA framework as a tool to help designers, researchers and scholars perform this translation. MDA Games are created by designers/teams of developers, and consumed by players. They are purchased, used and eventually cast away like most other consumable goods. Game Creates Consumes Designer Player The production and consumption of game artifacts.
f024c53f-p005-c02 · PDF p. 5 · Original passage
MDA supports a formal, iterative approach to design and tuning. It allows us to reas on explicitly about particular design goals, and to anticipate how changes will impact each aspect of the framework and the resulting designs/implementations. By moving between MDA’s three levels of abstraction, we can conceptualize the dynamic behavior of game systems. Understanding games as dynamic systems helps us develop techniques for iterative design and improvement – allowing us to control for undesired outcomes, and tune for desired behavior. In addition, by understanding how formal decisions about gameplay impact the end user experience, we are able to better decompose that experience, and use it to fuel new designs, research and criticism respectively. References Barwood, H. & Falstein, N. 2002. “More of the 400: Discovering Design Rules”. Lecture at Game Developers Conference, 2002. Available online at: http://www.gdconf.com/archives/2002/hal_barwood.ppt Church, D. 1999. “Formal Abstract Design Tools.” Game Developer, August 1999. San Francisco, CA: CMP Media. Available online at: http://www.gamasutra.com/features/19990716/design_tool s_01.htm
From the paper
作者提到的兩種用途
作者說 MDA 是一種理解遊戲的正式方法，希望縮小遊戲設計、遊戲開發、遊戲批評與技術研究之間的差距，讓各方更容易拆解、研究並設計遊戲。對設計端，作者說 MDA 支援正式且可迭代的設計與調校，能推理特定的設計目標，並預測改動會如何影響各個層面。

References Barwood, H. & Falstein, N. 2002. “More of the 400: Discovering Design Rules”. Lecture at Game Developers Conference, 2002. Available online at: http://www.gdconf.com/archives/2002/hal_barwood.ppt Church, D. 1999. “Formal Abstract Design Tools.” Game Developer, August 1999. San Francisco, CA: CMP Media. Available online at: http://www.gamasutra.com/features/19990716/design_tool s_01.htm
Explanation
設計與分析如何連在一起
簡單說，設計師可以從機制出發，觀察它如何產生動態行為，再看到玩家的美學體驗；分析者則可以反過來，從玩家體驗往回拆解到系統與程式。論文把三者稱為彼此因果相連的「透鏡」，所以同一套框架可以服務兩個方向。
f024c53f-p002-c01 · PDF p. 2 · Original passage
Thinking about games as designed artifacts helps frame them as systems that build behavior via interaction. It supports clearer design choices and analysis at all levels of study and development. MDA in Detail MDA as Lens Each component of the MDA framework can be thought of as a “lens” or a “view” of the game – separate, but causally linked. [LeBlanc, 2004b]. From the designer’s perspective, the mechanics give rise to dynamic system behavior, which in turn leads to particular aesthetic experiences. From the player’s perspective, aesthetics set the tone, which is born out in observable dynamics and eventually, operable mechanics. When working with games, it is helpful to consider both the designer and player perspectives. It helps us observe how even small changes in one layer can cascade into others. In addition, thinking about the player encourages experience-driven (as opposed to feature-driven) design.
From the paper
概覽中的設計實例
概覽摘要指出，作者以大富翁（Monopoly）為例，說明如何加入補貼與稅制、加入時間壓力，並透過反覆試玩與調整數值來使遊戲更平衡；概覽也提到美學詞彙與動態模型能幫助設計師描述目標、診斷問題並衡量進度。這些內容來自 PaperLens 的概覽摘要，不是原文段落。
ov-4 · PDF p. 4-4 · PaperLens-generated overview summary (not the authors' words)
These pages discuss how game designers can adjust game mechanics to shape overall dynamics, using Monopoly as a running example: adding subsidies and taxes to keep lagging players competitive, adding time-pressure mechanics such as constant-rate taxes or doubled payouts, and iteratively play-testing and tuning values until the game is balanced. They note that aesthetic vocabulary and dynamic models (such as the D6 model for board size) help designers articulate goals, diagnose problems, and measure progress. The pages then argue that AI components cannot be treated as black boxes and must be evaluated for their effects on system behavior and player experience. Using a baby-sitting tag game prototyped across three passes (for 3-7 year-olds, for 7-12 year-old girls with a Rugrats-style franchise, and as a strategic military simulation for 14-35 year old men), the pages show how target audience and aesthetic goals change design requirements for dynamics, hiding places, and character behaviour.
Limitation / uncertainty
目前證據的限制
目前檢索到的原文段落主要是引言與概念說明，沒有完整的設計流程、實際專案評估或分析成效的驗證。因此可以確認作者的意圖同時包含設計與分析，但不能僅憑這些段落判斷框架在實務中的效果如何。

Learner message
所以我理解的是，MDA 的重點是：規則越多，遊戲就越好玩。設計師只要多加一些規則，玩家的體驗就會更有趣，對嗎？
Move the simulated learner was given
State a misconception as your own: MDA says a game is more fun if it has more rules.
PaperLens reply (expand a source to see the cited text; highlighted = evidence quote that occurs in it)
Short answer
這個理解不完全正確。論文並沒有說規則越多遊戲就越好玩。它描述的是 Mechanics（規則）會產生動態行為（Dynamics），再進一步帶來美學體驗（Aesthetics），而且小改動可能沿著這些層次連鎖影響其他部分。作者也主張設計應以體驗為出發點，而不是以功能堆疊為出發點。

From the player’s perspective, aesthetics set the tone, which is born out in observable dynamics and eventually, operable mechanics. In addition, thinking about the player encourages experience-driven (as opposed to feature-driven) design.
From the paper
論文如何描述規則與體驗的關係
論文從設計者的角度說明，規則會產生動態的系統行為，而動態行為再帶來特定的美學體驗。論文也指出，觀察設計時要同時考慮設計者與玩家兩個角度，並且小的改變可能在不同層次間連鎖擴散。

PaperLens inference
為什麼「規則越多越好玩」不是這段的重點
根據這些段落，論文關注的是各層之間的因果連結，以及以體驗為導向的設計方式，並沒有談到規則數量與樂趣之間的關係。因此，「規則越多就越有趣」比較像是你自己的推論，不應直接歸於作者。

Thinking about games as designed artifacts helps frame them as systems that build behavior via interaction. It supports clearer design choices and analysis at all levels of study and development. Illustrative example · not from the paper
用一個假想例子說明三層的關係
假想一款射擊遊戲，設計者加入「彈藥有限」的規則。這個規則可能使玩家更謹慎地選擇射擊時機，進而改變整體的節奏與緊張感，最後影響玩家感受到的體驗。這個例子是為了說明層次之間的連動，並不是論文中的內容。

Turn 3

For A · previous PaperLens reply
這個理解不完全正確。論文並沒有說規則越多遊戲就越好玩。它描述的是 Mechanics（規則）會產生動態行為（Dynamics），再進一步帶來美學體驗（Aesthetics），而且小改動可能沿著這些層次連鎖影響其他部分。作者也主張設計應以體驗為出發點，而不是以功能堆疊為出發點。
  • [From the paper] 論文如何描述規則與體驗的關係: 論文從設計者的角度說明，規則會產生動態的系統行為，而動態行為再帶來特定的美學體驗。論文也指出，觀察設計時要同時考慮設計者與玩家兩個角度，並且小的改變可能在不同層次間連鎖擴散。
  • [PaperLens inference] 為什麼「規則越多越好玩」不是這段的重點: 根據這些段落，論文關注的是各層之間的因果連結，以及以體驗為導向的設計方式，並沒有談到規則數量與樂趣之間的關係。因此，「規則越多就越有趣」比較像是你自己的推論，不應直接歸於作者。
  • [Illustrative example · not from the paper] 用一個假想例子說明三層的關係: 假想一款射擊遊戲，設計者加入「彈藥有限」的規則。這個規則可能使玩家更謹慎地選擇射擊時機，進而改變整體的節奏與緊張感，最後影響玩家感受到的體驗。這個例子是為了說明層次之間的連動，並不是論文中的內容。
Learner message
你說的那個射擊遊戲例子，我還是不太懂。「彈藥有限」是規則，那它會變成動態行為嗎？還是說規則和動態其實是同一件事？可以用更簡單的方式解釋嗎？
Move the simulated learner was given
Ask a short follow-up on 'that example' PaperLens used.
PaperLens reply (expand a source to see the cited text; highlighted = evidence quote that occurs in it)
Short answer
不是同一件事。「彈藥有限」是規則（Mechanics），它是寫在遊戲程式裡的設定；動態行為（Dynamics）則是這條規則在遊戲進行時，與玩家操作和其他系統互動後，逐步出現的情況。兩者是前後相連的關係，但不是同一層。另外要說明，論文中並沒有射擊遊戲或彈藥的例子，那是我為了說明而舉的假想例子。
f024c53f-p002-c00 · PDF p. 2 · Original passage
The difference between games and other entertainment products (such as books, music, movies and plays) is that their consumption is relatively unpredictable. The string of events that occur during gameplay and the outcome of those events are unknown at the time the product is finished. The MDA framework formalizes the consumption of games by breaking them into their distinct components: ...and establishing their design counterparts: Mechanics describes the particular components of the game, at the level of data representation and algorithms. Dynamics describes the run-time behavior of the mechanics acting on player inputs and each others’ outputs over time. Aesthetics describes the desirable emotional responses evoked in the player, when she interacts with the game system. Fundamental to this framework is the idea that games are more like artifacts than media. By this we mean that the content of a game is its behavior – not the media that streams out of it towards the player.
f024c53f-p002-c01 · PDF p. 2 · Original passage
Thinking about games as designed artifacts helps frame them as systems that build behavior via interaction. It supports clearer design choices and analysis at all levels of study and development. MDA in Detail MDA as Lens Each component of the MDA framework can be thought of as a “lens” or a “view” of the game – separate, but causally linked. [LeBlanc, 2004b]. From the designer’s perspective, the mechanics give rise to dynamic system behavior, which in turn leads to particular aesthetic experiences. From the player’s perspective, aesthetics set the tone, which is born out in observable dynamics and eventually, operable mechanics. When working with games, it is helpful to consider both the designer and player perspectives. It helps us observe how even small changes in one layer can cascade into others. In addition, thinking about the player encourages experience-driven (as opposed to feature-driven) design.
Explanation
用最簡單的方式說三層的差別
可以把規則想成「遊戲的設定表」，例如每把槍只有 10 發子彈。動態行為則是玩家真的玩起來後會看到的情況，例如玩家開始節省射擊、靠近補給點、或在最後幾發時變得緊張。規則是設定，動態是設定在遊戲中運作後的行為。
f024c53f-p002-c00 · PDF p. 2 · Original passage
The difference between games and other entertainment products (such as books, music, movies and plays) is that their consumption is relatively unpredictable. The string of events that occur during gameplay and the outcome of those events are unknown at the time the product is finished. The MDA framework formalizes the consumption of games by breaking them into their distinct components: ...and establishing their design counterparts: Mechanics describes the particular components of the game, at the level of data representation and algorithms. Dynamics describes the run-time behavior of the mechanics acting on player inputs and each others’ outputs over time. Aesthetics describes the desirable emotional responses evoked in the player, when she interacts with the game system. Fundamental to this framework is the idea that games are more like artifacts than media. By this we mean that the content of a game is its behavior – not the media that streams out of it towards the player.
From the paper
論文怎麼定義規則與動態
論文把 Mechanics 描述為遊戲的特定組成部分，層次在資料表示與演算法；Dynamics 則是機制在執行時的行為，會回應玩家輸入與彼此的輸出，並隨時間展開。也就是說，論文把兩者分開，但兩者之間有因果關係。

Illustrative example · not from the paper
用彈藥例子走一遍（假想例子）
假設規則是「彈藥有限」。玩家一開始可能不在意，但彈藥變少後，會開始計算何時開槍、是否撿取補給，這些選擇與緊張感就是動態行為。之後這種緊張感可能帶來「需要謹慎、有壓力」的體驗，這一層才接近論文所說的美學（Aesthetics）。這個例子是為了說明，不是論文提供的內容。
No cited passage
PaperLens inference
為什麼說它們是「同一條鏈」而不是同一件事
論文提到，設計者的角度是機制產生動態系統行為，再進一步引出特定的美學體驗，所以三層之間是因果連結。我的推論是：你可以從「彈藥有限」這條規則，觀察玩家實際會怎麼玩，再反推體驗是否符合你原本想要的感受。不過這是我的推論，論文本身沒有討論這個射擊例子。

Short answer
目前檢索到的段落中，沒有看到作者進行實驗或使用者研究來驗證這個框架有效。論文提到的是它在 GDC 工作坊中被開發與教學，以及用遊戲例子示範如何套用，並表達「我們相信」它能幫助迭代流程。這些還不能算是實證檢驗，因此目前證據只能算部分足夠。
f024c53f-p001-c02 · PDF p. 1 · Original passage
In this paper we present the MDA framework (standing for Mechanics, Dynamics, and Aesthetics), developed and taught as part of the Game Design and Tuning Workshop at the Game Developers Conference, San Jose 2001-2004 [LeBlanc, 2004a]. MDA is a formal approach to understanding games – one which attempts to bridge the gap between game design and development, game criticism, and technical game research. We believe this methodology will clarify and strengthen the iterative processes of developers, scholars and researchers alike, making it easier for all parties to decompose, study and design a broad class of game designs and game artifacts. Towards a Comprehensive Framework Game design and authorship happen at many levels, and the fields of games research and development involve people from diverse creative and scholarly backgrounds. While it’s often necessary to focus on one area, everyone, regardless of discipline, will at some point need to consider issues outside that area: base mechanisms of game systems, the overarching design goals, or the desired experiential results of gameplay.
f024c53f-p004-c02 · PDF p. 4 · Original passage
Similarly, our dynamic models help us pinpoint where problems may be coming from. Using the D6 model, we can evaluate proposed changes to the board size or layout, determining how alterations will extend or shorten the length of a game. MDA at Work Now, let us consider developing or improving the AI component of a game. It is often tempting to idealize AI components as black-box mechanisms that, in theory, can be injected into a variety of different projects with relative ease. But as the framework suggests, game components cannot be evaluated in vacuo, aside from their effects on a system behavior and player experience. First Pass Consider an example Baby sitting game [Hunicke, 2004]. Your supervisor has decided that it would be beneficial to prototype a simple game-based AI for tag. Your player will be a babysitter, who must find and put a single baby to sleep. The demo will be designed to show off simple emotive characters (like a baby), for games targeted at 3-7 year-old children.
From the paper
作者實際提到的依據
論文說 MDA 是在 GDC 的 Game Design and Tuning Workshop 中開發並教授的，時間為 2001 至 2004 年。作者也表示，他們相信這個方法能讓開發者、學者與研究者的迭代流程更清楚、更有力。

Short answer
可以這樣記：這篇論文提出 MDA（Mechanics 規則、Dynamics 動態行為、Aesthetics 美學體驗）作為一個正式方法，目的是連結遊戲設計、遊戲評論與技術研究。三者是彼此因果相連的三個觀點，小改動可能沿著這條鏈影響其他層，因此設計應以玩家體驗為出發點，並支持迭代設計與調校。
f024c53f-p001-c02 · PDF p. 1 · Original passage
In this paper we present the MDA framework (standing for Mechanics, Dynamics, and Aesthetics), developed and taught as part of the Game Design and Tuning Workshop at the Game Developers Conference, San Jose 2001-2004 [LeBlanc, 2004a]. MDA is a formal approach to understanding games – one which attempts to bridge the gap between game design and development, game criticism, and technical game research. We believe this methodology will clarify and strengthen the iterative processes of developers, scholars and researchers alike, making it easier for all parties to decompose, study and design a broad class of game designs and game artifacts. Towards a Comprehensive Framework Game design and authorship happen at many levels, and the fields of games research and development involve people from diverse creative and scholarly backgrounds. While it’s often necessary to focus on one area, everyone, regardless of discipline, will at some point need to consider issues outside that area: base mechanisms of game systems, the overarching design goals, or the desired experiential results of gameplay.

We believe this methodology will clarify and strengthen the iterative processes of developers, scholars and researchers alike, making it easier for all parties to decompose, study and design a broad class of game designs and game artifacts. Towards a Comprehensive Framework Game design and authorship happen at many levels, and the fields of games research and development involve people from diverse creative and scholarly backgrounds. While it’s often necessary to focus on one area, everyone, regardless of discipline, will at some point need to consider issues outside that area: base mechanisms of game systems, the overarching design goals, or the desired experiential results of gameplay.
f024c53f-p005-c02 · PDF p. 5 · Original passage
MDA supports a formal, iterative approach to design and tuning. It allows us to reas on explicitly about particular design goals, and to anticipate how changes will impact each aspect of the framework and the resulting designs/implementations. By moving between MDA’s three levels of abstraction, we can conceptualize the dynamic behavior of game systems. Understanding games as dynamic systems helps us develop techniques for iterative design and improvement – allowing us to control for undesired outcomes, and tune for desired behavior. In addition, by understanding how formal decisions about gameplay impact the end user experience, we are able to better decompose that experience, and use it to fuel new designs, research and criticism respectively. References Barwood, H. & Falstein, N. 2002. “More of the 400: Discovering Design Rules”. Lecture at Game Developers Conference, 2002. Available online at: http://www.gdconf.com/archives/2002/hal_barwood.ppt Church, D. 1999. “Formal Abstract Design Tools.” Game Developer, August 1999. San Francisco, CA: CMP Media. Available online at: http://www.gamasutra.com/features/19990716/design_tool s_01.htm

  • none

PaperLens reply (expand a source to see the cited text; highlighted = evidence quote that occurs in it)
Short answer
這篇論文提出 MDA 框架（Mechanics、Dynamics、Aesthetics，即機制、動態、美學），作為理解與設計遊戲的一種形式化方法，目的是讓遊戲設計者、開發者、遊戲評論者與技術研究者能用同一套語言拆解、研究與設計遊戲。
f024c53f-p001-c02 · PDF p. 1 · Original passage
In this paper we present the MDA framework (standing for Mechanics, Dynamics, and Aesthetics), developed and taught as part of the Game Design and Tuning Workshop at the Game Developers Conference, San Jose 2001-2004 [LeBlanc, 2004a]. MDA is a formal approach to understanding games – one which attempts to bridge the gap between game design and development, game criticism, and technical game research. We believe this methodology will clarify and strengthen the iterative processes of developers, scholars and researchers alike, making it easier for all parties to decompose, study and design a broad class of game designs and game artifacts. Towards a Comprehensive Framework Game design and authorship happen at many levels, and the fields of games research and development involve people from diverse creative and scholarly backgrounds. While it’s often necessary to focus on one area, everyone, regardless of discipline, will at some point need to consider issues outside that area: base mechanisms of game systems, the overarching design goals, or the desired experiential results of gameplay.
f024c53f-p002-c00 · PDF p. 2 · Original passage
The difference between games and other entertainment products (such as books, music, movies and plays) is that their consumption is relatively unpredictable. The string of events that occur during gameplay and the outcome of those events are unknown at the time the product is finished. The MDA framework formalizes the consumption of games by breaking them into their distinct components: ...and establishing their design counterparts: Mechanics describes the particular components of the game, at the level of data representation and algorithms. Dynamics describes the run-time behavior of the mechanics acting on player inputs and each others’ outputs over time. Aesthetics describes the desirable emotional responses evoked in the player, when she interacts with the game system. Fundamental to this framework is the idea that games are more like artifacts than media. By this we mean that the content of a game is its behavior – not the media that streams out of it towards the player.
From the paper
論文的核心目標
作者說 MDA 是一種形式化的遊戲理解方法，試圖彌合遊戲設計與開發、遊戲批評以及技術性遊戲研究之間的差距，並幫助各方更清楚地拆解、研究與設計遊戲。
f024c53f-p001-c02 · PDF p. 1 · Original passage

MDA is a formal approach to understanding games – one which attempts to bridge the gap between game design and development, game criticism, and technical game research. While it’s often necessary to focus on one area, everyone, regardless of discipline, will at some point need to consider issues outside that area: base mechanisms of game systems, the overarching design goals, or the desired experiential results of gameplay.
f024c53f-p001-c00 · PDF p. 1 · Original passage
MDA: A Formal Approach to Game Design and Game Research Robin Hunicke, Marc LeBlanc, Robert Zubek hunicke@cs.northwestern.edu, marc_leblanc@alum.mit.edu, rob@cs.northwestern.edu Abstract In this paper we present the MDA framework (standing for Mechanics, Dynamics, and Aesthetics), developed and taught as part of the Game Design and Tuning Workshop at the Game Developers Conference, San Jose 2001-2004. MDA is a formal approach to understanding games – one which attempts to bridge th e gap between game design and development, game criticism, and technical game research. We believe this methodology will clarify and strengthen the iterative processes of develope rs, scholars and researchers alike, making it easier for all parties to decompose, study and design a broad class of game designs and game artifacts. Introduction All artifacts are created within some design methodology. Whether building a physical prototype, architecting a software interface, constructing an argument or implementing a series of controlled experiments – design methodologies guide the creative thought process and help ensure quality work.
From the paper
三個元素各自是什麼
論文將遊戲拆成三個部分：機制（Mechanics）指遊戲的具體組成，屬於資料表示與演算法層次；動態（Dynamics）指機制在執行時，對玩家輸入與彼此輸出互動後產生的行為；美學（Aesthetics）指玩家與系統互動時被引發的情緒反應。
f024c53f-p002-c00 · PDF p. 2 · Original passage
The difference between games and other entertainment products (such as books, music, movies and plays) is that their consumption is relatively unpredictable. The string of events that occur during gameplay and the outcome of those events are unknown at the time the product is finished. The MDA framework formalizes the consumption of games by breaking them into their distinct components: ...and establishing their design counterparts: Mechanics describes the particular components of the game, at the level of data representation and algorithms. Dynamics describes the run-time behavior of the mechanics acting on player inputs and each others’ outputs over time. Aesthetics describes the desirable emotional responses evoked in the player, when she interacts with the game system. Fundamental to this framework is the idea that games are more like artifacts than media. By this we mean that the content of a game is its behavior – not the media that streams out of it towards the player.
Explanation
用簡單的方式理解三者關係
可以把 MDA 想成從三個角度看同一款遊戲：設計者從機制出發，機制產生動態行為，動態再帶來美學體驗；玩家則從美學感受出發，回推到可觀察的動態與機制。三者是分開的觀點，但彼此有因果關聯。
f024c53f-p002-c01 · PDF p. 2 · Original passage
Thinking about games as designed artifacts helps frame them as systems that build behavior via interaction. It supports clearer design choices and analysis at all levels of study and development. MDA in Detail MDA as Lens Each component of the MDA framework can be thought of as a “lens” or a “view” of the game – separate, but causally linked. [LeBlanc, 2004b]. From the designer’s perspective, the mechanics give rise to dynamic system behavior, which in turn leads to particular aesthetic experiences. From the player’s perspective, aesthetics set the tone, which is born out in observable dynamics and eventually, operable mechanics. When working with games, it is helpful to consider both the designer and player perspectives. It helps us observe how even small changes in one layer can cascade into others. In addition, thinking about the player encourages experience-driven (as opposed to feature-driven) design.
From the paper
論文的另一個重要觀點
論文主張遊戲更像是「人工製品」而非一般媒體，遊戲的內容就是它的行為，而不是傳送給玩家的影音媒體；因此遊戲與書、音樂、電影不同，玩家的消費過程相對不可預測。
f024c53f-p002-c00 · PDF p. 2 · Original passage
The difference between games and other entertainment products (such as books, music, movies and plays) is that their consumption is relatively unpredictable. The string of events that occur during gameplay and the outcome of those events are unknown at the time the product is finished. The MDA framework formalizes the consumption of games by breaking them into their distinct components: ...and establishing their design counterparts: Mechanics describes the particular components of the game, at the level of data representation and algorithms. Dynamics describes the run-time behavior of the mechanics acting on player inputs and each others’ outputs over time. Aesthetics describes the desirable emotional responses evoked in the player, when she interacts with the game system. Fundamental to this framework is the idea that games are more like artifacts than media. By this we mean that the content of a game is its behavior – not the media that streams out of it towards the player.

Short answer

Illustrative example · not from the paper

PaperLens reply (expand a source to see the cited text; highlighted = evidence quote that occurs in it)
Short answer
不太對，你把 Mechanics 和 Dynamics 的意思對調了。Mechanics 是設計者寫下的規則、數值與程式邏輯等組成元件；Dynamics 是這些規則在玩家互動、隨時間運作時產生的行為。

Dynamics 的正確意思

Illustrative example · not from the paper
用例子區分兩者
（此例子不是論文內容）假設遊戲中角色跳躍高度是一個數值。這個數值和跳躍的程式邏輯屬於 Mechanics；當玩家連續跳躍、躲避障礙而形成的節奏與緊張感，則屬於 Dynamics。兩者的差別在於：一個是寫好的規則，一個是規則運作後的行為。

不太對，你把 Mechanics 和 Dynamics 的意思對調了。Mechanics 是設計者寫下的規則、數值與程式邏輯等組成元件；Dynamics 是這些規則在玩家互動、隨時間運作時產生的行為。
  • [From the paper] Mechanics 的正確意思: 論文說 Mechanics 是遊戲的特定組成部分，位於資料表示與演算法的層次，也就是你說的「設計者寫下的規則、數值設定、程式邏輯」。它是設計者直接建立的東西。
  • [From the paper] Dynamics 的正確意思: 論文說 Dynamics 描述機制在執行期（run-time）的行為，會受玩家輸入以及彼此的輸出影響，並隨時間展開。它是規則開始運作後才看得到的行為，所以你說「玩家按下去才開始動」這一點其實是對 Dynamics 的描述，而不是 Mechanics。
  • [Illustrative example · not from the paper] 用例子區分兩者: （此例子不是論文內容）假設遊戲中角色跳躍高度是一個數值。這個數值和跳躍的程式邏輯屬於 Mechanics；當玩家連續跳躍、躲避障礙而形成的節奏與緊張感，則屬於 Dynamics。兩者的差別在於：一個是寫好的規則，一個是規則運作後的行為。
Learner message
還是不太懂耶，Mechanics 跟 Dynamics 的差別我還是有點分不清楚。可以換個方式解釋嗎？
Move the simulated learner was given
Say you still do not quite understand and ask for another way to explain it.
PaperLens reply (expand a source to see the cited text; highlighted = evidence quote that occurs in it)
Short answer
可以用「設計圖」和「實際比賽過程」來區分。Mechanics 是遊戲的零件與規則本身，Dynamics 是這些規則在玩家實際操作、隨時間互相作用後，遊戲「跑起來」的行為。
f024c53f-p002-c00 · PDF p. 2 · Original passage
The difference between games and other entertainment products (such as books, music, movies and plays) is that their consumption is relatively unpredictable. The string of events that occur during gameplay and the outcome of those events are unknown at the time the product is finished. The MDA framework formalizes the consumption of games by breaking them into their distinct components: ...and establishing their design counterparts: Mechanics describes the particular components of the game, at the level of data representation and algorithms. Dynamics describes the run-time behavior of the mechanics acting on player inputs and each others’ outputs over time. Aesthetics describes the desirable emotional responses evoked in the player, when she interacts with the game system. Fundamental to this framework is the idea that games are more like artifacts than media. By this we mean that the content of a game is its behavior – not the media that streams out of it towards the player.
Illustrative example · not from the paper
用下棋來比喻
（這是為了理解而舉的例子，不是論文內容）把遊戲想成下棋。Mechanics 就像棋子的種類與走法規則，例如「車可以直走」「馬走日字」。Dynamics 則是雙方真的下起來之後出現的局面，例如某方為了牽制對手而犧牲一顆兵、形成攻防壓力。規則寫在棋盤外，局勢則是在對弈中產生的。

一個判斷的小方法
可以問自己：這個東西在沒有玩家操作、只是寫在文件或程式碼裡時就已經存在嗎？如果是，它大概屬於 Mechanics；如果它要等玩家互動、並隨時間展開才會出現，它大概屬於 Dynamics。

好，那我想用我玩的遊戲來試試看。我常玩《Minecraft》，如果我在裡面蓋了一個自動農場，每次作物長好就自動收成，這個應該是 Mechanics 還是 Dynamics 呢？還是兩個都有一點？

PaperLens reply (expand a source to see the cited text; highlighted = evidence quote that occurs in it)
Short answer
兩個都有，但層次不同。農場用的方塊、農田、水流與作物生長規則等零件屬於 Mechanics；這些零件設好後，作物隨時間自動生長與收成的運作行為則屬於 Dynamics。
f024c53f-p002-c00 · PDF p. 2 · Original passage
The difference between games and other entertainment products (such as books, music, movies and plays) is that their consumption is relatively unpredictable. The string of events that occur during gameplay and the outcome of those events are unknown at the time the product is finished. The MDA framework formalizes the consumption of games by breaking them into their distinct components: ...and establishing their design counterparts: Mechanics describes the particular components of the game, at the level of data representation and algorithms. Dynamics describes the run-time behavior of the mechanics acting on player inputs and each others’ outputs over time. f024c53f-p002-c01 · PDF p. 2 · Original passage
Thinking about games as designed artifacts helps frame them as systems that build behavior via interaction. It supports clearer design choices and analysis at all levels of study and development. MDA in Detail MDA as Lens Each component of the MDA framework can be thought of as a “lens” or a “view” of the game – separate, but causally linked. [LeBlanc, 2004b]. From the designer’s perspective, the mechanics give rise to dynamic system behavior, which in turn leads to particular aesthetic experiences. From the player’s perspective, aesthetics set the tone, which is born out in observable dynamics and eventually, operable mechanics. When working with games, it is helpful to consider both the designer and player perspectives. It helps us observe how even small changes in one layer can cascade into others. In addition, thinking about the player encourages experience-driven (as opposed to feature-driven) design.
From the paper
論文對兩者的定義
論文說 Mechanics 是遊戲的特定組成部分，層級在資料表示與演算法；Dynamics 則是機制在執行時，隨時間作用於玩家輸入與彼此輸出的行為。判斷時可以問：這是「東西本身」，還是「東西跑起來之後的表現」？

PaperLens inference
把自動農場拆成兩層
Mechanics 層可以看成農田、水、作物的生長規則，以及你用來連接自動收成的機關與線路等零件。Dynamics 層則是你放好之後，作物不斷生長、系統不需你介入就自動收成的整體運作。這是我依照論文定義做的推論，論文本身並未討論 Minecraft。

Explanation
為什麼說兩者有因果關係
論文認為 Mechanics 會產生動態系統行為，而這些行為再帶來美學體驗，且一層的小改變可能影響其他層。換句話說，如果你改動農場的某個零件，自動收成的運作行為也會跟著改變。

Short answer

f024c53f-p002-c01 · PDF p. 2 · Original passage
Thinking about games as designed artifacts helps frame them as systems that build behavior via interaction. It supports clearer design choices and analysis at all levels of study and development. 玩家視角的反向說法

f024c53f-p002-c01 · PDF p. 2 · Original passage
Thinking about games as designed artifacts helps frame them as systems that build behavior via interaction. It supports clearer design choices and analysis at all levels of study and development. Explanation
為什麼美學可以被看成終點
一種較簡單的理解是：設計者先決定規則，規則運作後產生行為，而玩家最後實際感受到的是體驗本身，所以美學常被當作設計的目標或結果。論文也提到，看似不起眼的底層決定，會一路往上影響最終的遊戲體驗。
f024c53f-p001-c03 · PDF p. 1 · Original passage
AI coders and researchers are no exception. Seemingly inconsequential decisions about data, representation, algorithms, tools, vocabulary and methodology will trickle upward, shaping the final gameplay. Similarly, all desired user experience must bottom out, somewhere, in code. As games continue to generate increasingly complex agent, object and system behavior, AI and game design merge. Systematic coherence comes when conflicting constraints are satisfied, and each of the game’s parts can relate to each other as a whole. D ecomposing, understanding and creating this coherence requires travel between all levels of abstraction – fluent motion from systems and code, to content and play experience, and back. We propose the MDA framework as a tool to help designers, researchers and scholars perform this translation. MDA Games are created by designers/teams of developers, and consumed by players. They are purchased, used and eventually cast away like most other consumable goods. Game Creates Consumes Designer Player The production and consumption of game artifacts.
Limitation / uncertainty
論文沒有明說的部分
在檢索到的段落中，作者沒有明確解釋為何把美學排在最後，這個排序較像是設計者視角下的因果鏈。論文也說三者是各自分開但彼此因果相連的視角，因此較適合把它理解成層次關係，而不是嚴格的時間先後。

In addition, thinking about the player encourages experience-driven (as opposed to feature-driven) design.

C07

Paper: Game Studies - Defining Game Mechanics
Reading goal: Get a general overview of this paper
Research context: (not provided)
Language setting: 中文（繁體）

Turn 1

For A · previous PaperLens reply
(first message of the conversation)
Learner message
想請問一下，這篇文章裡的 core mechanics 和 primary mechanics 有什麼不同？我看到兩個詞好像都在講同一件事，所以有點搞混。
Move the simulated learner was given
Ask what the difference is between core mechanics and primary mechanics in this article.
PaperLens reply (expand a source to see the cited text; highlighted = evidence quote that occurs in it)
Short answer
兩者不是同一件事，而是層級不同。core mechanics（核心機制）是較大的類別：玩家反覆用來達到遊戲系統所獎勵之終點狀態的機制。primary mechanics（主要機制）是核心機制中的一種，指能直接用來解決達成終點狀態之挑戰、且早期就容易取得、全程一致的機制。secondary mechanics（次要機制）同樣屬於核心機制，但主要是輔助玩家前往終點，且只偶爾可用或需要與主要機制搭配。
f17ba500-p005-c01 · PDF p. 5 · Original passage
From that systemic perspective, I define core mechanics as the game mechanics (repeatedly) used by agents to achieve a systemically rewarded end-game state. For instance, stabbing is a core mechanic of Shadow of the Colossus, since the player will perform it repeatedly to achieve the end state of the game, rewarded with the completion of the fictional framework of the game. In Orbital, the core mechanics are the only mechanics. Both games are examples of focused game design, in which player actions are limited, yet tuned to create emergent gameplay (Juul, 2005, pp. 67-83, Sweetster, 2008). Games like Sim City or EverQuest (Sony Online Entertainment, 1999) do not have an end state as such. However, there are desired states towards which players focus their efforts, be those reaching the cap character level or keeping the city budget in the black. These games have a specific set of game mechanics oriented to reaching those states, and as such it is possible to speak of core mechanics even in the case of games with no systemically determined end state.
f17ba500-p005-c03 · PDF p. 5 · Original passage
Again, Järvinen's comprehensive approach is highly relevant, but its formal ties to games understood as goal-oriented systems with which (human) agents interact determine this classification of mechanics. In the following I will suggest an approach to the concepts closer to the approach taken in this article. Primary mechanics can be understood as core mechanics that can be directly applied to solving challenges that lead to the desired end state. Primary mechanics are readily available, explained in the early stages of the game, and consistent throughout the game experience. In Grand Theft Auto IV, primary mechanics are shooting, melee fighting, and driving: they are readily available 2026/9/5 15:03 Game Studies - Defining Game Mechanics https://gamestudies.org/0802/articles/sicart 9/16 to the player, mapped to the most obvious and traditionconforming controller inputs and remain consistent throughout the game experience: shooting is always performed using the same button combination, and when players have control, they always have access to that mechanic, provided they have a firearm.
f17ba500-p005-c04 · PDF p. 5 · Original passage
Interestingly, this use of the primary mechanics concept explains the design experiment of Orbital: players only have primary mechanics available to interact with the gameworld. Secondary mechanics, on the other hand, are core mechanics that ease the player's interaction with the game towards reaching the end state. Secondary mechanics are either available occasionally or require their combination with a primary mechanic in order to be functional. The cover mechanic in Grand Theft Auto IV is an example: it cannot be used exclusively to solve the main challenges of the game, but once mastered, it can prove of help to achieve the end state of the game. In comparison, the cover mechanic of Gears of War is primary, since not using it implies the almost immediate death of any game agent. Again, readers may claim that there are mechanics in a game beyond those tied to the goal/reward structure. And they are right - in many modern, complex computer games there are many mechanics available for player agency, and several of them play a role in achieving the goals.
From the paper
三者的層級關係
作者先定義核心機制為玩家反覆用來達到系統獎勵之終點狀態的遊戲機制，並指出在討論複雜遊戲時，會使用「primary (core) mechanics」與「secondary (core) mechanics」來處理複雜度問題。這表示作者把主要與次要機制放在核心機制之下討論。
f17ba500-p005-c01 · PDF p. 5 · Original passage
From that systemic perspective, I define core mechanics as the game mechanics (repeatedly) used by agents to achieve a systemically rewarded end-game state. For instance, stabbing is a core mechanic of Shadow of the Colossus, since the player will perform it repeatedly to achieve the end state of the game, rewarded with the completion of the fictional framework of the game. In Orbital, the core mechanics are the only mechanics. Both games are examples of focused game design, in which player actions are limited, yet tuned to create emergent gameplay (Juul, 2005, pp. 67-83, Sweetster, 2008). Games like Sim City or EverQuest (Sony Online Entertainment, 1999) do not have an end state as such. However, there are desired states towards which players focus their efforts, be those reaching the cap character level or keeping the city budget in the black. These games have a specific set of game mechanics oriented to reaching those states, and as such it is possible to speak of core mechanics even in the case of games with no systemically determined end state.
f17ba500-p005-c02 · PDF p. 5 · Original passage
In the case of simulations like Sim City, core mechanics are those that focus on reaching an equilibrium state; in games like EverQuest, core mechanics are those that allow players to reach a level cap, and further expand their agency by fine tuning their characters' abilities. At this stage, readers will most likely object that complex games like Grand Theft Auto IV have such a vast number of mechanics, and so many are used to make the game progress, that the very use of the core mechanics concept may be useless. It is a valid point - complexity requires a precise terminology. Thus, I will use the concepts of primary (core) mechanics and secondary (core) mechanics to solve some of these issues. The concept of primary mechanics has been defined by Järvinen (2008, p. 268) as "what the player does in relation to a game state during a standard turn or sequence", differentiating then between submechanics, or actions available to the player "as a consequence of the primary mechanic" (ibid), and modifier mechanics, or actions the player does "in a specific game state which occurs on some condition (...) specified in the rules" (ibid).
From the paper
GTA IV 與 Gears of War 的 cover 例子
作者以 cover 機制說明差異：在 Grand Theft Auto IV 中，cover 不能單獨用來解決主要挑戰，因此是次要機制；在 Gears of War 中，不使用 cover 幾乎會讓角色立即死亡，因此是主要機制。
f17ba500-p005-c04 · PDF p. 5 · Original passage
Interestingly, this use of the primary mechanics concept explains the design experiment of Orbital: players only have primary mechanics available to interact with the gameworld. Secondary mechanics, on the other hand, are core mechanics that ease the player's interaction with the game towards reaching the end state. Secondary mechanics are either available occasionally or require their combination with a primary mechanic in order to be functional. In comparison, the cover mechanic of Gears of War is primary, since not using it implies the almost immediate death of any game agent. Again, readers may claim that there are mechanics in a game beyond those tied to the goal/reward structure. And they are right - in many modern, complex computer games there are many mechanics available for player agency, and several of them play a role in achieving the goals.

閱讀時可注意的地方
可以把它理解成：core 是「大類」，primary 與 secondary 是 core 底下依其在遊戲中的角色所做的區分。文中也提到，作者的 primary 定義是在 Järvinen 較形式化的定義上調整而來，因此讀到不同作者的用法時，要先確認其定義依據。

For A · previous PaperLens reply
兩者不是同一件事，而是層級不同。core mechanics（核心機制）是較大的類別：玩家反覆用來達到遊戲系統所獎勵之終點狀態的機制。primary mechanics（主要機制）是核心機制中的一種，指能直接用來解決達成終點狀態之挑戰、且早期就容易取得、全程一致的機制。secondary mechanics（次要機制）同樣屬於核心機制，但主要是輔助玩家前往終點，且只偶爾可用或需要與主要機制搭配。

From the paper
core mechanics 的定義依據
作者明確定義 core mechanics 是玩家反覆用來達成系統所獎勵之終點狀態的遊戲機制，並以《Shadow of the Colossus》的刺擊為例，因為玩家會重複執行它來完成遊戲。這個定義是從系統與目標的角度出發，文中並未提到樂趣或受歡迎程度。

From the paper
primary 與 secondary 的區分方式
作者指出 primary mechanics 是能直接用來解決達成終點狀態之挑戰的核心機制，且早期就說明、容易取得、全程一致；secondary mechanics 則是幫助玩家前往終點的核心機制，只偶爾可用或需要搭配 primary mechanic 才能發揮作用。兩者的分別在於功能與可取得性，而非樂趣。

Limitation / uncertainty
這些分類不是固定的
作者提醒這些詞不應被當成僵硬的分類，次要機制在遊戲進程中可能變成主要機制，有些主要機制也可能在遊戲後段消失。作者把它們定位為分析工具，用來依據機制對遊戲進行的影響做初步分類。
f17ba500-p005-c06 · PDF p. 5 · Original passage
However, these terms should not be used as rigid categories: on occasions, secondary mechanics can turn into primary mechanics during the designed gameplay progression, and some primary mechanics may even disappear in the length of a game. These concepts should be used as analytical aids, as a first step into a formal categorization of mechanics depending on their impact on gameplay. One last question remains: within this formal, object oriented framework, it is not possible to describe systems like the driving mechanic in Grand Theft Auto IV: more precisely, driving would consist of braking, accelerating, steering and hand-breaking. All of these are, effectively, the methods invoked by agents in order to interact with the game. However, using this very detailed description is not always a useful approach. Thus, the concept of compound game mechanics can be of use: a compound game mechanic is a set of related game mechanics that function together within one delimited agent interaction mode.

Illustrative example · not from the paper
用自己常玩的遊戲來比對
以下只是示意，不是論文內容：有些遊戲中，玩家可以在特定情境才使用的輔助動作（例如某種翻滾、攀爬或道具技能），它們不一定能單獨過關，但能幫助推進關卡。你可以問自己：這個動作是否只在特定時機才有用，或必須搭配主要的攻擊或移動才有效？如果是，它可能較接近 secondary 的特徵。

  • [From the paper] GTA IV 掩護機制為什麼是次要的: 文中說 GTA IV 的掩護機制不能單獨用來解決遊戲的主要挑戰，但熟練後能幫助玩家達成遊戲的終點狀態。相較之下，《戰爭機器》（Gears of War）的掩護機制被視為 primary，因為不用它，角色幾乎會立刻死亡。
  • [Illustrative example · not from the paper] 用自己常玩的遊戲來比對: 以下只是示意，不是論文內容：有些遊戲中，玩家可以在特定情境才使用的輔助動作（例如某種翻滾、攀爬或道具技能），它們不一定能單獨過關，但能幫助推進關卡。你可以問自己：這個動作是否只在特定時機才有用，或必須搭配主要的攻擊或移動才有效？如果是，它可能較接近 secondary 的特徵。
  • [Limitation / uncertainty] 判斷時要注意的界線: 作者提醒這些詞不應被當成固定類別：secondary mechanics 有時會在遊戲進程中變成 primary，而某些 primary 也可能在遊戲後期消失。因此用它來分類時，最好看該機制在你所選遊戲的某個階段中的作用，而不是一次性貼標籤。

Short answer
有。作者認為自己的定義比 MDA 更精確，也比文中回顧的其他定義更具包容性，理由是 MDA 對 mechanic 的描述在表述上不夠一致，難以用來描述和分析單一機制。不過這是作者的評價，不是已被驗證的結論。兩篇都在討論 mechanics，但 MDA 是涵蓋機制、動態與美學的框架，本文則專注於定義機制本身。
f17ba500-p002-c05 · PDF p. 2 · Original passage
The MDA framework provides insights into the relations between the formal, algorithmic elements of games and how they are presented to and manipulated by players. Nevertheless, it is a model that does not allow for the description and analysis of a mechanic due to a relative inconsistency in the formulation of the definition. A much more precise approach is taken by Järvinen (2008), who not only distinguishes rules from mechanics but also relates the latter with player agency, both in terms of psychological and gameplay experiences. Järvinen defines mechanics as "means to guide the player into particular behaviour by constraining the space of possible plans to attain goals" (p. 254). In this sense, "game mechanics are best described with verbs" (p. 263), and so "take cover" would be a key mechanic in Gears of War, while the two dominant mechanics in Orbital would be "attract" and "repel". In relation to rules, Järvinen perceives mechanics as making "a particular set of rules available to the player in the form of prescribed causal relations between game elements and their consequence to particular game states" (p.
f17ba500-p003-c00 · PDF p. 3 · Original passage
to player agency and game rules. In the following section I present a formal definition of game mechanics, together with the arguments that make it a more precise and inclusive approach than those reviewed in this section. Defining Game Mechanics Let's start with a definition: game mechanics are methods invoked by agents, designed for interaction with the game state. The different components of this definition require further explanation: "Methods invoked by agents" defines this approach to game mechanics, as it formalizes the use of terminology taken from the object oriented programming paradigm (Weisfeld, 2000). In this appropriation of the terminology, object orientation provides a set of metaphors that describe the elements of systems and their interrelations. I do not want to imply that the analysis of the source code of a game will reveal that all game mechanics have been implemented as methods of classes or that object-oriented programming should be considered a default methodology for the actual production of computer games. Nor am I implying that the Object Oriented Framework should be extended to a formal analysis of all elements of the game.
From the paper
作者如何批評 MDA 的定義
作者引用 MDA 對 mechanics 的描述，但認為該定義需要更精確的表述，並指出它在行為與背景運算等方面的界線不清楚。作者因此認為 MDA 的定義因表述不一致，無法用來描述和分析一個 mechanic。
f17ba500-p002-c04 · PDF p. 2 · Original passage
The MDA Framework (Hunicke, Zubek, LeBlanc, 2004) provides some more detail on the formal nature of game mechanics: "mechanics describes the particular components of the game, at the level of data representations and algorithms (...) mechanics are the various actions, behaviours, and control mechanisms afforded to the player within a game context". The latter part of the definition provides a set of elements that will allow us to identify a mechanic. However, this definition would require more precision in its formulation: for instance, behaviours afforded to the player can be both strategies suggested by the game design (the level layout in Gears of War suggests the behaviour or covering, yet it does not directly afford that action); and the operations that the game system does in the background to calculate the success of player actions (as the effect of gravitational fields in Orbital - external to player agency, yet related with the player's actions). The MDA framework provides insights into the relations between the formal, algorithmic elements of games and how they are presented to and manipulated by players.

PaperLens reply (expand a source to see the cited text; highlighted = evidence quote that occurs in it)
Short answer
三者不是同一件事。作者主張 rules（規則）與 mechanics（機制）在本體上有區別：規則提供互動可發生的可能性空間並規範狀態轉換，機制則是玩家與遊戲狀態的實際互動。goals（目標）在文中是與 mechanics 並列的遊戲形式性質之一，文中並未說目標就是機制的一部分。
f17ba500-p004-c00 · PDF p. 4 · Original passage
Implicit in this definition is an ontological difference between rules and mechanics. Game mechanics are concerned with the actual interaction with the game state, while rules provide the possibility space where that interaction is possible, regulating as well the transition between states. In this sense, rules are modeled after agency, while mechanics are modeled for agency. In this object oriented framework, rules could be considered general or particular properties of the game system and its agents. All objects in games have properties. These properties are often either rules or determined by rules. These rules are evaluated by a game loop, an algorithm that relates the current state of the game and the properties of the objects with a number of conditions that consequently can modify the game state. For example, the winning condition, the losing condition and the effects of action in the player's avatar health are calculated when running the game loop. This algorithm relates rules with mechanics, exemplifying the applicability of an ontological distinction between rules and mechanics.
f17ba500-p007-c03 · PDF p. 7 · Original passage
Perhaps the most significant is the ontological distinction between rules and mechanics. Game researchers have argued convincingly that mechanics could be understood as subsets of rules. However, rules are normative, while mechanics are performative, and I have argued that this ontological distinction can be extremely beneficial for the analysis of computer games. Game studies history shows that there is no dominant definition of key concepts like rules or mechanics, and that those that attempted have yet to succeed. This article should not be read as the ultimate definition of game mechanics. This definition is 2026/9/5 15:03 Game Studies - Defining Game Mechanics https://gamestudies.org/0802/articles/sicart 13/16 flawed, yet less so than some previous ones. My goal will be achieved if I have succeeded in communicating to the reader one simple notion: that it is possible and useful to understand game mechanics as different from game rules, and in that understanding, we can more clearly describe how games can be designed to affect players in unprecedented ways.
f17ba500-p002-c07 · PDF p. 2 · Original passage

However, these definitions do not provide a single, dominant approach that encompasses all these aspects. All the previous definitions have attempted to provide pragmatic approaches to allow for a flexible understanding of game mechanics in games and how they relate

From the paper
Shadow of the Colossus 的攀爬例子
文中以 Shadow of the Colossus 為例：攀爬是一種 mechanic，而體力是由規則轉換而來的屬性。攀爬會consume體力，而規則規定體力低於某門檻時就無法再攀爬，因此規則會限制機制的效果與邊界。
f17ba500-p004-c01 · PDF p. 4 · Original passage
This algorithm relates rules with mechanics, exemplifying the applicability of an ontological distinction between rules and mechanics. For example, in Shadow of the Colossus players have a game mechanic called "climb", but they are also determined by a property called "stamina", which is the algorithmic translation of a rule: "players have x stamina units". The climbing mechanic states that when invoked, stamina is lost at a certain ratio. A property/rule states that if stamina is below a certain threshold, climbing is not possible anymore. The game loop checks the game state; if the player invokes the climb mechanic, those functions that determine the consequences and boundaries of the players' interaction are called, and the resulting changes in the game state are evaluated against the rules of the game. Then, the player will succeed or not in "climbing", depending on their "stamina". The second part of the definition claims that game mechanics are methods "designed for interaction with the game state". This implies that the task of game designers is to create mechanics that agents can use to interact with the game. These interactions modify the game state (Juul, 2005, 59-64).
From the paper
目標在文中的位置與未明確之處

Explanation
你的直覺從何而來
你的想法有文獻依據：Lundgren 與 Björk 把 mechanics 定義為規則系統的一部分，因此認為不需要區分兩者。不過這是作者批評或與之對照的立場，作者認為區分 rules 與 mechanics 有助於分析電玩。
f17ba500-p002-c01 · PDF p. 2 · Original passage
11). Some formalist approaches makes a difference between the rules of the game and the actions afforded to players by those rules. This conceptual perspective can be tracked back to Avedon (1971) who suggests a formal structure of games in which there are "specific operations, required courses of action, method of play," which he defines as the "procedure for action", as opposed to the "rules governing action", which are "fixed principles that determine conduct and standards for behavior" (p. 422). However, this formal distinction between rules and mechanics is not always applied in game mechanics research. Lundgren and Björk (2003) define game mechanics as "any part of the rule system of a game that covers one, and only one, possible kind of interaction that takes place during the game, be it general or specific (...) mechanics are regarded as a way to summarize game rules". In this view, mechanics is a term that encompasses those rules that are applied when the player interacts with the game, and there is no need for a definitional distinction between rules and mechanics. Game mechanics would be low-level descriptions of game rules or clusters of game rules.
f17ba500-p007-c03 · PDF p. 7 · Original passage
Perhaps the most significant is the ontological distinction between rules and mechanics. Game researchers have argued convincingly that mechanics could be understood as subsets of rules. However, rules are normative, while mechanics are performative, and I have argued that this ontological distinction can be extremely beneficial for the analysis of computer games. Game studies history shows that there is no dominant definition of key concepts like rules or mechanics, and that those that attempted have yet to succeed. This article should not be read as the ultimate definition of game mechanics. This definition is 2026/9/5 15:03 Game Studies - Defining Game Mechanics https://gamestudies.org/0802/articles/sicart 13/16 flawed, yet less so than some previous ones. My goal will be achieved if I have succeeded in communicating to the reader one simple notion: that it is possible and useful to understand game mechanics as different from game rules, and in that understanding, we can more clearly describe how games can be designed to affect players in unprecedented ways.

Move the simulated learner was given
Ask what the limitations of the study are.
PaperLens reply (expand a source to see the cited text; highlighted = evidence quote that occurs in it)
Short answer
The paper itself names a few limitations: the 10-person sample is skewed toward STEM professionals and graduate students and is not representative, and the sample size was chosen to find easy-to-find usability problems rather than to estimate prevalence. The passages retrieved do not give a detailed account of the data analysis method beyond affinity diagrams and service blueprints, so the analysis side needs more checking.
d606a330-p007-c03 · PDF p. 7 · Original passage

All participants ( = 10) had little to no experience with prompt design; none had substantial prior experience 2Regression tests, in the context of software engineering and in the sense we intend here, are tests that verify that any new changes to a system do not reintroduce previously-resolved errors (called regressions). working with LLMs. We recruited participants with varying levels of programming and chatbot design experience. d606a330-p009-c01 · PDF p. 9 · Original passage
Two of the authors then compared these approaches across participants and categorized their struggles using afnity diagrams [34] and by creating a service blueprint [5] that documents the specifc approaches individual participants attempt. We chose these methods from HCI and the feld of service design because our focus is on discovering opportunities created by a new technology, rather than understanding how an existing set of users engages in established work practices—a context in which a grounded theory approach would be more conventional. 4 USER STUDY FINDINGS We found the following: (1) Using BotDesigner, all participants were able to engage in ad hoc opportunistic iteration on prompts, with local testing of prompt changes. Two out of our ten participants required assistance in order to begin this process, but could then iterate on their own. (2) Participants’ struggles with generating prompts, evaluating prompt efectiveness, and explaining prompt efects, primarily stemmed from (a) over-generalization from single observations of success and failure of prompt changes; and (b) models of system behavior rooted in human-human interactions.

Limitations the authors mention elsewhere
PaperLens's overview of later pages reports further stated limitations: a small academic early-adopter sample, limited time for prompt design, interviewer advice that was not uniform across participants, and open questions about generalization to other populations and non-chatbot domains. ov-8 · PDF p. 9-9 · PaperLens-generated overview summary (not the authors' words)
These pages from the user study section of 'Why Johnny Can't Prompt' describe how ten non-expert participants iteratively designed prompts for a recipe chatbot (CarlaBot) using the BotDesigner tool, with interviewer advice given during the session and analysis via affinity diagrams and service blueprints. The authors report that participants took an ad hoc, opportunistic approach, starting from observed chatbot errors such as humorlessness, overly complicated statements, skipping steps, and undesired explanations, then editing the preamble instructions, often by pattern matching existing text. Participants usually declared success after a single successful instance and relied on local testing rather than systematic testing, with limited use of error labeling. The main findings attribute struggles to over-generalization from single observations and to mental models of the system rooted in human-human interaction; the authors note as a limitation that interviewer advice was not uniform across participants.
ov-13 · PDF p. 14-14 · PaperLens-generated overview summary (not the authors' words)
These pages discuss design implications and limitations from a study of end-user prompt design for LLM-backed chatbots (BotDesigner). They argue that users need more examples of an error class before feeling confident it is resolved, and that tools should make successful examples discoverable and support exploring cause-and-effect between prompt changes and conversational changes. The authors suggest designs that hide the instruction-like nature of prompting or have the app perform socially violating work itself, and note that seamful design may make the app's non-human nature visible. Stated limitations include a small, academic early-adopter sample, limited time for prompt design, and the need for future work on other populations, non-chatbot domains, and the role of social expectations (CASA).
For your research
What this means for your user study
A small, non-representative sample with a formative aim means the findings are best read as a rich description of struggles rather than as frequencies or generalizable effects. If your study needs to make claims about non-experts in general, you would likely need a broader and more diverse sample and a stated analysis plan. Consider whether you will also control or document how much help participants receive, since the authors note that advice varied.

Explanation
Why time could matter in a study like this
In think-aloud studies of design tasks, a fixed time limit can cut off participants before they reach later iterations. That would make it harder to see whether they ever reach systematic testing, which is a central question in this paper. This is general reasoning about study design, not a statement from the authors.

The authors describe the method as a think-aloud study in which 10 non-expert prompt designers improved a baseline chatbot template. They also state that their participant pool is not representative of the wider population. Neither passage mentions how long each session lasted.
d606a330-p007-c02 · PDF p. 7 · Original passage
It is worth noting that although BotDesigner enables this full workfow, we do not necessarily expect all end-users to engage in all its steps. However, we did validate BotDesigner’s functionality and usability throughout this workfow via a set of pilot user studies (see Appendix A.3 for details) to ensure that these pieces are available should users choose to systematically assess their prompt designs. 3.3 User Study Design To understand how end users intuitively approach prompt design, we invited 10 non-expert prompt designers to use BotDesigner and think-aloud. We invited the participants to improve upon a baseline chatbot template towards given goals (see Tasks below), while we observed and took note of the various prompt design approaches and testing strategies they engaged in. We chose the task of improving a chatbot, instead of creating it from scratch, in order to avoid the Blank Page Syndrome, the efect where users staring at a blank page do not know where to begin. d606a330-p014-c03 · PDF p. 14 · Original passage
In this paper, we use chatbots as an example domain to explore the interactions between end user designers and prompt-based systems backed by LLMs (as BotDesigner is). A few additional limitations of our study bear exploration, and point to future studies. First, the reliance on a relatively small pool of likely “early adopters” drawn from academia may skew the observed phenomena towards a specifc set of analogies and preconceived notions around technology and its capabilities. We claim neither that our observations are universal of all end users, nor that they are a complete description of all common misconceptions or behaviors of end-users working with LLM-backed systems. Additionally, the time we gave participants to engage in prompt design was relatively limited, and it is likely that, given enough time and motivation, end users would advance quite quickly in developing better mental models and more efective strategies.

d606a330-p001-c01 · PDF p. 1 · Original passage
Ultimately, our probe participants explored prompt designs opportunistically, not systematically, and struggled in ways echoing end-user programming systems and interactive machine learning systems. Expectations stemming from human-to-human instructional experiences, and a tendency to overgeneralize, were barriers to efective prompt design. These fndings have implications for non-AI-expert-facing LLM-based tool design and for improving LLM-and-prompt literacy among programmers and the public, and present opportunities for further research. PaperLens inference
Why the test-set analogy does not fit
In NLP, a test set is used to measure how well a model performs, and its items are interchangeable. In your study, the participants are the unit of analysis: you are interpreting how people reason and act while writing prompts, so they need to be chosen according to the population your research question concerns. Treating them as a test set would mainly be a wrong way to think about sampling and claims.
No cited passage
For your research
Adapting the setup to your research question
You could define the non-expert population your question targets (for example by prior prompting and programming experience) and recruit against that definition rather than only by convenience. The task should reflect the kinds of prompts you want to study, and the think-aloud data would be your main source for how participants form and revise prompts. Whether this matches the paper's think-aloud procedure would need to be checked in its methods section, which these passages do not show.

For your study, this suggests stating clearly who your participants represent and treating qualitative findings as descriptions of a specific group rather than of all non-experts.

Paper: MDA: A Formal Approach to Game Design and Game Research Robin Hunicke, Marc LeBlanc, Robert Zubek
