Move the simulated learner was given
Ask in a broad way what this paper is about.
PaperLens reply (expand a source to see the cited text; highlighted = evidence quote that occurs in it)
Short answer
這篇論文提出 MDA 框架（Mechanics 機制、Dynamics 動態、Aesthetics 美學），作為理解與設計遊戲的正式方法，希望幫助遊戲設計、遊戲評論與技術遊戲研究之間的溝通與迭代流程。
f024c53f-p001-c02 · PDF p. 1 · Original passage
In this paper we present the MDA framework (standing for Mechanics, Dynamics, and Aesthetics), developed and taught as part of the Game Design and Tuning Workshop at the Game Developers Conference, San Jose 2001-2004 [LeBlanc, 2004a]. MDA is a formal approach to understanding games – one which attempts to bridge the gap between game design and development, game criticism, and technical game research. We believe this methodology will clarify and strengthen the iterative processes of developers, scholars and researchers alike, making it easier for all parties to decompose, study and design a broad class of game designs and game artifacts. Towards a Comprehensive Framework Game design and authorship happen at many levels, and the fields of games research and development involve people from diverse creative and scholarly backgrounds. While it’s often necessary to focus on one area, everyone, regardless of discipline, will at some point need to consider issues outside that area: base mechanisms of game systems, the overarching design goals, or the desired experiential results of gameplay.
f024c53f-p002-c00 · PDF p. 2 · Original passage
The difference between games and other entertainment products (such as books, music, movies and plays) is that their consumption is relatively unpredictable. The string of events that occur during gameplay and the outcome of those events are unknown at the time the product is finished. The MDA framework formalizes the consumption of games by breaking them into their distinct components: ...and establishing their design counterparts: Mechanics describes the particular components of the game, at the level of data representation and algorithms. Dynamics describes the run-time behavior of the mechanics acting on player inputs and each others’ outputs over time. Aesthetics describes the desirable emotional responses evoked in the player, when she interacts with the game system. Fundamental to this framework is the idea that games are more like artifacts than media. By this we mean that the content of a game is its behavior – not the media that streams out of it towards the player.
From the paper
MDA 三個組成部分的定義
論文把遊戲拆成三個部分：Mechanics 指遊戲的具體元件，位於資料表示與演算法層次；Dynamics 指機制在執行時，面對玩家輸入與彼此輸出後產生的行為；Aesthetics 指玩家與遊戲系統互動時，被引發的期望情緒反應。
f024c53f-p002-c00 · PDF p. 2 · Original passage
The difference between games and other entertainment products (such as books, music, movies and plays) is that their consumption is relatively unpredictable. The string of events that occur during gameplay and the outcome of those events are unknown at the time the product is finished. The MDA framework formalizes the consumption of games by breaking them into their distinct components: ...and establishing their design counterparts: Mechanics describes the particular components of the game, at the level of data representation and algorithms. Dynamics describes the run-time behavior of the mechanics acting on player inputs and each others’ outputs over time. Aesthetics describes the desirable emotional responses evoked in the player, when she interacts with the game system. Fundamental to this framework is the idea that games are more like artifacts than media. By this we mean that the content of a game is its behavior – not the media that streams out of it towards the player.
Explanation
三者如何互相連動
可以把 MDA 想成三個看遊戲的「透鏡」。從設計者角度，機制產生動態系統行為，進而帶來特定的美學體驗；從玩家角度，美學先定下調性，再體現在可觀察的動態中，最後回到可操作的機制。
f024c53f-p002-c01 · PDF p. 2 · Original passage
Thinking about games as designed artifacts helps frame them as systems that build behavior via interaction. It supports clearer design choices and analysis at all levels of study and development. MDA in Detail MDA as Lens Each component of the MDA framework can be thought of as a “lens” or a “view” of the game – separate, but causally linked. [LeBlanc, 2004b]. From the designer’s perspective, the mechanics give rise to dynamic system behavior, which in turn leads to particular aesthetic experiences. From the player’s perspective, aesthetics set the tone, which is born out in observable dynamics and eventually, operable mechanics. When working with games, it is helpful to consider both the designer and player perspectives. It helps us observe how even small changes in one layer can cascade into others. In addition, thinking about the player encourages experience-driven (as opposed to feature-driven) design.
From the paper
論文的目的與背景
作者認為遊戲與書籍、音樂、電影不同，因為玩家的消費過程比較不可預測。論文希望 MDA 能橫跨遊戲設計與開發、遊戲評論，以及技術遊戲研究，讓不同領域的人都能拆解、研究並設計遊戲。
f024c53f-p001-c02 · PDF p. 1 · Original passage
In this paper we present the MDA framework (standing for Mechanics, Dynamics, and Aesthetics), developed and taught as part of the Game Design and Tuning Workshop at the Game Developers Conference, San Jose 2001-2004 [LeBlanc, 2004a]. MDA is a formal approach to understanding games – one which attempts to bridge the gap between game design and development, game criticism, and technical game research. We believe this methodology will clarify and strengthen the iterative processes of developers, scholars and researchers alike, making it easier for all parties to decompose, study and design a broad class of game designs and game artifacts. Towards a Comprehensive Framework Game design and authorship happen at many levels, and the fields of games research and development involve people from diverse creative and scholarly backgrounds. While it’s often necessary to focus on one area, everyone, regardless of discipline, will at some point need to consider issues outside that area: base mechanisms of game systems, the overarching design goals, or the desired experiential results of gameplay.
f024c53f-p002-c00 · PDF p. 2 · Original passage
The difference between games and other entertainment products (such as books, music, movies and plays) is that their consumption is relatively unpredictable. The string of events that occur during gameplay and the outcome of those events are unknown at the time the product is finished. The MDA framework formalizes the consumption of games by breaking them into their distinct components: ...and establishing their design counterparts: Mechanics describes the particular components of the game, at the level of data representation and algorithms. Dynamics describes the run-time behavior of the mechanics acting on player inputs and each others’ outputs over time. Aesthetics describes the desirable emotional responses evoked in the player, when she interacts with the game system. Fundamental to this framework is the idea that games are more like artifacts than media. By this we mean that the content of a game is its behavior – not the media that streams out of it towards the player.
ov-2 · PDF p. 2-2 · PaperLens-generated overview summary (not the authors' words)
These pages introduce the MDA framework (Mechanics, Dynamics, Aesthetics) for analysing and designing games, contrasting games with other entertainment products because their consumption is unpredictable. The framework treats games as artifacts whose content is their behavior, and defines Mechanics as data representations and algorithms, Dynamics as run-time behavior, and Aesthetics as desired emotional responses. Each component is presented as a lens causally linked to the others, with designer and player perspectives explained and experience-driven design favored over feature-driven design. The pages then move to a taxonomy of eight aesthetic categories (Sensation, Fantasy, Narrative, Challenge, Fellowship, Discovery, Expression, Submission), illustrated with examples of Charades, Quake, The Sims and Final Fantasy, and note there is no formula for what produces fun.
From the paper
論文中的應用例子
概覽顯示，論文以 Monopoly（大富翁）為例，說明回饋循環如何拉大玩家差距，並提出補貼落後者、對領先者加稅等調整；也以 AI 追逐遊戲原型，說明目標受眾與美學目標如何改變設計需求。這些是概覽的摘要，不是原文逐字內容。
ov-3 · PDF p. 3-3 · PaperLens-generated overview summary (not the authors' words)
These pages introduce a taxonomy-based approach to describing games, using aesthetics, dynamics, and mechanics as a framework for understanding player appeal. They present aesthetic models, illustrated with competitive games like Charades and Quake, which rely on adversarial play and clear feedback about who is winning. The dynamics section explains how elements such as time pressure, information sharing, item building, and rising tension produce experiences like challenge, fellowship, expression, and dramatic tension. Using Monopoly as the main example, the pages describe how feedback loops can widen gaps between players and erode engagement, and suggest design fixes such as catch-up rewards. The pages do not state formal limitations beyond noting that realism may conflict with fun.
ov-4 · PDF p. 4-4 · PaperLens-generated overview summary (not the authors' words)
These pages discuss how game designers can adjust game mechanics to shape overall dynamics, using Monopoly as a running example: adding subsidies and taxes to keep lagging players competitive, adding time-pressure mechanics such as constant-rate taxes or doubled payouts, and iteratively play-testing and tuning values until the game is balanced. They note that aesthetic vocabulary and dynamic models (such as the D6 model for board size) help designers articulate goals, diagnose problems, and measure progress. The pages then argue that AI components cannot be treated as black boxes and must be evaluated for their effects on system behavior and player experience. Using a baby-sitting tag game prototyped across three passes (for 3-7 year-olds, for 7-12 year-old girls with a Rugrats-style franchise, and as a strategic military simulation for 14-35 year old men), the pages show how target audience and aesthetic goals change design requirements for dynamics, hiding places, and character behaviour.

PaperLens reply (expand a source to see the cited text; highlighted = evidence quote that occurs in it)
Short answer
簡單來說：Mechanics 是遊戲的組成零件（規則、資料與演算法）；Dynamics 是這些零件在遊戲執行時、與玩家輸入及彼此互動後產生的行為；Aesthetics 是玩家在與遊戲互動時所產生的情感反應。
f024c53f-p002-c00 · PDF p. 2 · Original passage
The difference between games and other entertainment products (such as books, music, movies and plays) is that their consumption is relatively unpredictable. The string of events that occur during gameplay and the outcome of those events are unknown at the time the product is finished. The MDA framework formalizes the consumption of games by breaking them into their distinct components: ...and establishing their design counterparts: Mechanics describes the particular components of the game, at the level of data representation and algorithms. Dynamics describes the run-time behavior of the mechanics acting on player inputs and each others’ outputs over time. Aesthetics describes the desirable emotional responses evoked in the player, when she interacts with the game system. Fundamental to this framework is the idea that games are more like artifacts than media. By this we mean that the content of a game is its behavior – not the media that streams out of it towards the player.
Explanation
三個詞的白話解釋
可以把 Mechanics 想成「遊戲的零件與規則」，Dynamics 想成「遊戲開始跑之後，這些零件怎麼互相作用、隨時間產生的變化」，Aesthetics 則是「玩家玩的時候心裡的感受，例如好玩、緊張、有成就感」。

From the paper
三者的關係與方向
論文指出三者是彼此分開、但有因果連結的「透鏡」。從設計者角度看，機制產生動態行為，動態再帶出美學體驗；從玩家角度看，美學設定基調，並體現在可觀察的動態與最終可操作的機制中。

Short answer
你的理解前後有點顛倒。依論文定義，Mechanics 是遊戲的組成零件（資料表示與演算法層級的規則與結構）；Dynamics 則是這些機制在遊戲執行時，受玩家輸入與彼此輸出影響、隨時間產生的行為。所以「按跳躍鍵角色會跳起」比較接近 Mechanics，「連續跳躍時節奏慢慢形成」比較接近 Dynamics。

f024c53f-p004-c00 · PDF p. 4 · Original passage
For example, the mechanics of card games include shuffling, trick-taking and betting – from which dynamics like bluffing can emerge. The mechanics of shooters include weapons, ammunition and spawn points – which sometimes produce things like camping and sniping. The mechanics of golf include balls, clubs, sand traps and water hazards – which sometimes produce broken or drowned clubs. Adjusting the mechanics of a game helps us fine-tune the game’s overall dynamics. Consider our Monopoly example. Mechanics that would help lagging players could include bonuses or “subsidies” for poor players, and penalties or “taxes” for rich players – perhaps calculated when crossing the Go square, leaving jail, or exercising monopolies over a certain threshold in value. By applying such changes to the fundamental rules of play, we might be able to keep lagging players competitive and interested for longer periods of time.

Dynamics describes the run-time behavior of the mechanics acting on player inputs and each others’ outputs over time. Aesthetics describes the desirable emotional responses evoked in the player, when she interacts with the game system. Fundamental to this framework is the idea that games are more like artifacts than media. By this we mean that the content of a game is its behavior – not the media that streams out of it towards the player.
f024c53f-p004-c00 · PDF p. 4 · Original passage
For example, the mechanics of card games include shuffling, trick-taking and betting – from which dynamics like bluffing can emerge. The mechanics of shooters include weapons, ammunition and spawn points – which sometimes produce things like camping and sniping. The mechanics of golf include balls, clubs, sand traps and water hazards – which sometimes produce broken or drowned clubs. Adjusting the mechanics of a game helps us fine-tune the game’s overall dynamics. Consider our Monopoly example. Mechanics that would help lagging players could include bonuses or “subsidies” for poor players, and penalties or “taxes” for rich players – perhaps calculated when crossing the Go square, leaving jail, or exercising monopolies over a certain threshold in value. By applying such changes to the fundamental rules of play, we might be able to keep lagging players competitive and interested for longer periods of time.
Illustrative example · not from the paper
用你玩的遊戲來區分
以射擊遊戲為例：子彈數量、重生點、武器種類都是 Mechanics，因為它們是設計者寫進遊戲的東西。玩家為了不被發現而躲在草叢或角落等待敵人，這種「反覆出現的走位與埋伏」則比較像 Dynamics，因為它是規則跟玩家操作一起運作後才慢慢出現的行為。（這是我舉的例子，不是論文中的內容。）

Explanation
一個判斷問題
你可以問自己兩個問題：「這是設計者明確定下的規則或結構嗎？」如果是，較接近 Mechanics。「這是玩家在多次遊玩中，因為規則互相作用而反覆看到的行為模式嗎？」如果是，較接近 Dynamics。論文也用卡牌遊戲說明：洗牌、吃牌、下注是機制，從中可能浮現「虛張聲勢」這種動態。

From the paper
論文原文怎麼說
論文把 Mechanics 描述為遊戲在資料表示與演算法層級的組成部分，把 Dynamics 描述為機制在執行時，受玩家輸入與彼此輸出影響、隨時間產生的行為。論文也指出，機制的調整會影響整體動態，例如射擊遊戲的重生點是機制，而可能因此出現的「露營」與「狙擊」則屬於動態。

For A · previous PaperLens reply
可以用一個簡單的區分來想：Mechanics 是設計者放進遊戲裡的「零件」（規則與結構），Dynamics 是這些零件在遊戲進行時，跟玩家操作和彼此互動後，「一段時間內反覆出現的行為模式」。前者是你能直接指出來的規則，後者是你在玩的過程中觀察到的結果。

Learner message
我平常玩《英雄聯盟》，裡面每個英雄都有自己的技能冷卻時間，還有擊殺後會拿到金錢可以買裝備。我想問：冷卻時間和買裝備這些，是屬於 Mechanics、Dynamics，還是 Aesthetics 呢？我還是有點分不清楚，可以用我的例子幫我分一下嗎？
Move the simulated learner was given
Bring an example from a game you play and ask which of the three parts it belongs to.
PaperLens reply (expand a source to see the cited text; highlighted = evidence quote that occurs in it)
Short answer
依照論文的定義，冷卻時間和擊殺後拿金錢買裝備比較接近 Mechanics，因為它們是遊戲的規則與資源結構。玩家因此安排技能時機、決定買哪件裝備所形成的局勢起伏，比較接近 Dynamics；玩家看到裝備成形後的緊張或成就感，則屬於 Aesthetics。這是 PaperLens 依定義做的推論，論文本身並沒有討論《英雄聯盟》。
f024c53f-p002-c00 · PDF p. 2 · Original passage
The difference between games and other entertainment products (such as books, music, movies and plays) is that their consumption is relatively unpredictable. The string of events that occur during gameplay and the outcome of those events are unknown at the time the product is finished. The MDA framework formalizes the consumption of games by breaking them into their distinct components: ...and establishing their design counterparts: Mechanics describes the particular components of the game, at the level of data representation and algorithms. Dynamics describes the run-time behavior of the mechanics acting on player inputs and each others’ outputs over time. Aesthetics describes the desirable emotional responses evoked in the player, when she interacts with the game system. Fundamental to this framework is the idea that games are more like artifacts than media. By this we mean that the content of a game is its behavior – not the media that streams out of it towards the player.
PaperLens inference
冷卻時間與買裝備屬於哪一層
冷卻時間、擊殺拿金錢、買裝備，都是事先寫進遊戲裡的規則與資源結構，因此可以歸為 Mechanics。論文說 Mechanics 是遊戲的「particular components」，也就是零件層級的東西。

哪些現象屬於 Dynamics

哪些感受屬於 Aesthetics

Learner message
你剛才說的第三個，為什麼作者要把它放在最後？是因為它是前面機制和動態的結果嗎？
Move the simulated learner was given
Refer to 'the third one' with a short reference and ask why the authors put it last.
PaperLens reply (expand a source to see the cited text; highlighted = evidence quote that occurs in it)
Short answer
你的理解有一半對：從設計者的角度看，論文確實把順序寫成機制產生動態、動態再帶出美學體驗。但作者並沒有說明為何把 Aesthetics 放在名稱的最後；而且他們討論時是先談 Aesthetics，再談 Dynamics，最後才談 Mechanics。
f024c53f-p002-c01 · PDF p. 2 · Original passage
Thinking about games as designed artifacts helps frame them as systems that build behavior via interaction. It supports clearer design choices and analysis at all levels of study and development. MDA in Detail MDA as Lens Each component of the MDA framework can be thought of as a “lens” or a “view” of the game – separate, but causally linked. [LeBlanc, 2004b]. From the designer’s perspective, the mechanics give rise to dynamic system behavior, which in turn leads to particular aesthetic experiences. From the player’s perspective, aesthetics set the tone, which is born out in observable dynamics and eventually, operable mechanics. When working with games, it is helpful to consider both the designer and player perspectives. It helps us observe how even small changes in one layer can cascade into others. In addition, thinking about the player encourages experience-driven (as opposed to feature-driven) design.
f024c53f-p002-c02 · PDF p. 2 · Original passage
As such, we begin our investigation with a discussion of Aesthetics, and continue on to Dynamics, finishing with the underlying Mechanics. Aesthetics What makes a game “fun”? How do we know a specific type of fun when we see it? Talking about games and play is hard because the vocabulary we use is relatively limited. In describing the aesthetics of a game, we want to move away from words like “fun” and “gameplay” towards a more directed vocabulary. This includes but is not limited to the taxonomy listed here: For example, consider the games Charades, Quake, The Sims and Final Fantasy. While each are “fun” in their own right, it is much more informative to consider the aesthetic components that create their respective player experiences: Charades: Fellowship, Expression, Challenge. Quake: Challenge, Sensation, Competition, Fantasy. The Sims: Discovery, Fantasy, Expression, Narrative. Final Fantasy: Fantasy, Narrative, Expression, Discovery, Challenge, Submission.
From the paper
設計者視角的因果鏈
論文從設計者的角度描述：機制產生動態系統行為，動態再引出特定的美學體驗。因此在這個方向上，Aesthetics 確實位於前面兩者的結果端。

PaperLens inference
為什麼名稱順序和討論順序不同（推論）
PaperLens 的推論是：名稱 MDA 反映的是設計者從規則到體驗的製作流程，而作者討論時先從 Aesthetics 開始，可能是因為他們想先釐清玩家應該感受到什麼。這一點論文沒有明確說明，只是我根據上下文的推斷。
No cited passage
Explanation
三者是視角而非單向流程
論文說 MDA 的每個部分都是遊戲的一種「透鏡」或「視角」，彼此分開但因果相連。所以不必把 Aesthetics 只看成最後一步，它也可以作為設計目標來反推需要哪些動態與機制。

In addition, thinking about the player encourages experience-driven (as opposed to feature-driven) design.

C09 · criterion A
C09 · criterion B

C10

Paper: Why Johnny Can't Prompt: How Non-AI Experts Try (and Fail) to Design LLM Prompts
Reading goal: Explore potential use in my research
Research context: I am a PhD student in NLP moving into HCI. My project studies how non-expert users write prompts for LLM-based tools, and I plan to run a user study.
Language setting: English

Turn 1

For A · previous PaperLens reply
(first message of the conversation)
Learner message
Hi, so I want to ask about the part where participants had trouble generalizing from examples. According to the paper, why did they struggle with that? Is it because the examples were too few, like a small training set? I am an NLP person so I think of it like few-shot learning.
Move the simulated learner was given
Ask why participants struggled to generalize from examples, according to the paper.
PaperLens reply (expand a source to see the cited text; highlighted = evidence quote that occurs in it)
Short answer
Not quite. The paper does not attribute the struggle to too few examples. Participants had trouble seeing how an example's prompt design applied to their own domain, so they often judged examples too far from their goal and did not try them. The paper also does not test the number of examples, so the few-shot framing does not match its findings.
d606a330-p011-c05 · PDF p. 11 · Original passage
The universality of the advice “show, don’t tell” [46] for novice writers, suggesting that humans commonly reach for descriptions over examples, is another possible corroboration for this bias. Seeking Help Two participants (P2, P4) who did seek solutions on the web both had a hard time making use of the examples they found, in part because they could not see how specifc prompt designs translated across domains. P2, for example, searched Google for “how can GPT- 3 assume an identity, ” hoping to fnd advice on having their chatbot take on Carla’s identity. Despite fnding an example illustrating how to generate text written in the style of a particular author, P2 decided this example was too far from what they were looking for, and did not attempt that particular approach. One participant, P6, didn’t pursue seeking solutions on the web because “I have no idea what I would Google because I’ve never worked with language models before. ” (P6) 4.3.2 Evaluating Prompts. Although all participants struggled with generating prompts in various ways, they were all ultimately successful in adding and modifying prompt text in ways that led to some, if not all, of the desired efects.
d606a330-p010-c00 · PDF p. 10 · Original passage
CHI ’23, April 23–28, 2023, Hamburg, Germany Zamfirescu-Pereira, Wong, Hartmann & Yang the “retry” button on the specifc problematic utterance the participant was trying to resolve, or by starting new conversations. 4.2 Challenges in End-User Prompt Design Though we report on a wide spectrum of our participants’ struggles iterating on prompts, we found that many of these behaviors arose from two fundamental sources: over-generalization from limited experience, and a social lens that fltered participants’ prompts (and the system’s responses to those prompts) through expectations originating in human-human interactions. First, participants over-generalized from single data points, whether positive or negative. For example, when writing a prompt to generate specifc behavior, participants often stopped iterating once the behavior was observed in a single conversational context, without considering other conversations or contexts—or gave up too early if the behavior was not observed on a frst or second attempt, and assumed the system was incapable of generating that behavior.

CHI ’23, April 23–28, 2023, Hamburg, Germany Zamfirescu-Pereira, Wong, Hartmann & Yang the “retry” button on the specifc problematic utterance the participant was trying to resolve, or by starting new conversations. 4.2 Challenges in End-User Prompt Design Though we report on a wide spectrum of our participants’ struggles iterating on prompts, we found that many of these behaviors arose from two fundamental sources: over-generalization from limited experience, and a social lens that fltered participants’ prompts (and the system’s responses to those prompts) through expectations originating in human-human interactions. For example, when writing a prompt to generate specifc behavior, participants often stopped iterating once the behavior was observed in a single conversational context, without considering other conversations or contexts—or gave up too early if the behavior was not observed on a frst or second attempt, and assumed the system was incapable of generating that behavior.
Explanation
How this differs from few-shot learning
In few-shot prompting, the model learns from examples placed in the prompt, and the question is how many and which examples help. The paper's difficulty is on the human side: users had to decide whether an example's prompt pattern transferred to their own task, and they often could not make that judgment. The paper does not report the number of examples people saw or any effect of example count.
d606a330-p011-c05 · PDF p. 11 · Original passage
The universality of the advice “show, don’t tell” [46] for novice writers, suggesting that humans commonly reach for descriptions over examples, is another possible corroboration for this bias. For your research
Possible use in your user study
You could separate two variables: how many examples users see, and how relevant or transferable those examples appear to them. Measuring whether users judge an example as applicable, and whether they try it, could connect to the paper's observation that participants rejected examples that seemed too far from their goal. This is a suggestion for you to weigh, and the design choice remains yours.

Limitation / uncertainty
Limits of this evidence
The passages come from a small qualitative study with a limited number of participants and do not include quantitative tests of example count. The few-shot comparison is therefore your interpretation rather than the authors' claim. The paper does say that a searchable repository of examples with the contexts where they work could help users, which is a design suggestion rather than a tested result.
d606a330-p013-c03 · PDF p. 13 · Original passage
A searchable repository of examples, such as The DALL·E 2 Prompt Book [37], could help users overcome the “design”, “selection”, and “use” barriers that often led to the aforementioned dead ends. In particular, these examples should include specifc prompts that work, and the contexts in which they work, so users can set their own expectations around when to try what kind of instruction, or what kind of sample dialog (or other input-output pair) to include, how much to repeat themselves, how much emotion to give, etc. Examples would also help users develop reasonable expectations about whether a specifc type of prompt design should work in a given context—and if not, whether rephrasing or repositioning that prompt would help. Third, in our related work, we identifed a few prompt design approaches that recent literature supports as efective, including the use of example input/output pairs (such as sample conversational turns, in the context of chatbots) and the use of repetition within prompts. We found, however, that some users avoided these known-efective strategies, sometimes even when encouraged by the interviewer to try them.

For your research
Possible use in your user study
You could separate in your study whether non-experts reject an example at first sight, attempt adaptation and fail, or avoid example-based strategies altogether, and record the reason they give for each. Short answer
Not quite. The retrieved passages describe BotDesigner as a design probe used to observe how non-experts intuitively design prompts, not as a baseline that participants' prompts are compared against. They do not describe any comparison condition, so the baseline framing is not supported by these passages.
d606a330-p004-c01 · PDF p. 4 · Original passage
We chose to create a design probe because the robustness of prompt strategies is highly dependent on specifc conversational contexts, as is users’ ability to create prompts and debug. Enabling people to engage in designing LLM-and-prompt-based chatbots hands-on offers deeper insights than conducting interviews about hypothetical scenarios alone. We chose to purpose-build a prompt design tool because existing tools focus on enabling non-experts to experiment with various prompting strategies rather than crafting robust and generalizable prompts. 3.1 Designing a No-Code Prompt Design Tool as Probe Design Goals. We have two goals in designing BotDesigner: First, to enable end-users, without any prompt design experience or even programming or ML experience, to (i) create a chatbot using prompts, without code, and (ii) systematically evaluate their prompts. Second, to allow end-users to engage in this process in a fexible, open-ended way, allowing us to observe their intuitive behaviors and thought processes.
d606a330-p003-c05 · PDF p. 3 · Original passage
3 METHOD: DESIGN PROBE In this work, we aim to understand how non-experts intuitively approach designing robust prompts, and whether and how they struggle in doing so. We want to dive deep into what those struggles reveal about people’s assumptions and understanding of promptbased systems in order to inform how end-user-facing prompt design tools might best support their users. We chose to investigate these questions in the context of designing instructional chatbots—chatbots that walk users through an activity (e.g., cooking a recipe, fxing a wif connection) while answering user questions and engaging in social conversation as needed. Chatbot tasks are sufciently open-ended that they open the door to a wide variety of types of problems and prompt-based solution approaches. Instructional chatbots represent a common chatbot use case (e.g., customer service bots), and their constituent tasks are some of the tasks that GPT-3 peforms more efectively out-of-the-box.
From the paper
What the probe was for
The authors say they built a no-code prompt design tool as a probe so that end-users could engage in designing chatbots hands-on. Their stated aim was to let end-users work in a flexible, open-ended way so the authors could observe intuitive behaviour and thought processes.
d606a330-p004-c01 · PDF p. 4 · Original passage

d606a330-p009-c01 · PDF p. 9 · Original passage
Two of the authors then compared these approaches across participants and categorized their struggles using afnity diagrams [34] and by creating a service blueprint [5] that documents the specifc approaches individual participants attempt. We chose these methods from HCI and the feld of service design because our focus is on discovering opportunities created by a new technology, rather than understanding how an existing set of users engages in established work practices—a context in which a grounded theory approach would be more conventional. 4 USER STUDY FINDINGS We found the following: (1) Using BotDesigner, all participants were able to engage in ad hoc opportunistic iteration on prompts, with local testing of prompt changes. Two out of our ten participants required assistance in order to begin this process, but could then iterate on their own. (2) Participants’ struggles with generating prompts, evaluating prompt efectiveness, and explaining prompt efects, primarily stemmed from (a) over-generalization from single observations of success and failure of prompt changes; and (b) models of system behavior rooted in human-human interactions.
Explanation
Probe versus baseline
A baseline in an NLP paper is a reference system that the proposed method is measured against on the same task. A design probe, as described here, is a research instrument that makes people's assumptions and strategies visible, so the focus is on what participants do and why. Some probes can be used in comparisons, but the retrieved passages do not describe this paper doing so.
No cited passage
For your research
Possible use in your user study
If your study needs a comparison, you would likely need a separate condition, such as a baseline interface or an existing prompt tool, defined and justified in your own design. The paper's probe could inspire how you give participants a hands-on tool, but it would not by itself give you a control. You make the final decision on this.
No cited passage
Limitation / uncertainty
Limits of this evidence
The passages retrieved so far do not describe the study's experimental design beyond the probe and the qualitative analysis. The authors' sample of ten participants and their exploratory aim also mean the paper does not test whether the probe performs better than any alternative.

Why Johnny Can’t Prompt CHI ’23, April 23–28, 2023, Hamburg, Germany After this initial phase, again in the interest of observing subsequent behavior, we would ofer suggestions for prompts to try that were in line with the research around prompt design. Examples of this advice include “try reframing that ‘do not’ statement into a positive statement, say what it is you want the bot to say, not what you don’t want it to say” or “have you considered copying some of the Carla-Lizzie transcript directly into the preamble?” We did not intentionally vary this advice across participants; however, due to the unpredictable nature of which errors participants chose to focus on, not all participants received the same advice or advice in the same order, a limitation of our approach. Analysis & Evaluation. How the data were analysed
The authors describe the analysis as exploratory: they transcribed all videos and observed which prompt design approaches participants attempted. Two of the authors then compared approaches across participants and categorised struggles with affinity diagrams and a service blueprint. They explicitly contrast this with grounded theory, which they say would be more conventional for an existing work practice.
d606a330-p009-c00 · PDF p. 9 · Original passage
Why Johnny Can’t Prompt CHI ’23, April 23–28, 2023, Hamburg, Germany After this initial phase, again in the interest of observing subsequent behavior, we would ofer suggestions for prompts to try that were in line with the research around prompt design. Examples of this advice include “try reframing that ‘do not’ statement into a positive statement, say what it is you want the bot to say, not what you don’t want it to say” or “have you considered copying some of the Carla-Lizzie transcript directly into the preamble?” We did not intentionally vary this advice across participants; however, due to the unpredictable nature of which errors participants chose to focus on, not all participants received the same advice or advice in the same order, a limitation of our approach. Analysis & Evaluation. We asked participants to think aloud, then engaged in exploratory data analysis, transcribing all videos and observing what prompt design approaches participants attempted, and noting when participants appeared to struggle and where interviewers intervened.
d606a330-p009-c01 · PDF p. 9 · Original passage
Two of the authors then compared these approaches across participants and categorized their struggles using afnity diagrams [34] and by creating a service blueprint [5] that documents the specifc approaches individual participants attempt. We chose these methods from HCI and the feld of service design because our focus is on discovering opportunities created by a new technology, rather than understanding how an existing set of users engages in established work practices—a context in which a grounded theory approach would be more conventional. 4 USER STUDY FINDINGS We found the following: (1) Using BotDesigner, all participants were able to engage in ad hoc opportunistic iteration on prompts, with local testing of prompt changes. Two out of our ten participants required assistance in order to begin this process, but could then iterate on their own. (2) Participants’ struggles with generating prompts, evaluating prompt efectiveness, and explaining prompt efects, primarily stemmed from (a) over-generalization from single observations of success and failure of prompt changes; and (b) models of system behavior rooted in human-human interactions.
Limitation / uncertainty
What is missing for NLP-style rigour
The retrieved passages do not mention a test set, a number of coders, an inter-annotator agreement score, or a codebook procedure. The passages also note that the advice given to participants was not varied intentionally, so it was not identical across participants. Because the passages found so far do not cover these points, I cannot confirm the coding reliability.

Paper: Hunyuan3D 2.0: Scaling Diffusion Models for High Resolution Textured 3D Assets Generation
Reading goal: Explore potential use in my research

For A · previous PaperLens reply
(first message of the conversation)
Learner message
好，我先大致了解了一下这篇论文的整体框架。那我想先问 shape 部分：论文里的 shape model 是怎么表示和编码 3D shape 的？是用 voxel、point cloud 还是 implicit representation（比如 SDF 或 occupancy）？另外它的 latent 是怎么得到的，用的是 VAE 还是别的结构？
Move the simulated learner was given
Ask about how the shape model represents and encodes 3D shapes.
PaperLens reply (expand a source to see the cited text; highlighted = evidence quote that occurs in it)
Short answer
根据已检索到的段落，Hunyuan3D 2.0 的 shape model 不是用 voxel 或 point cloud 直接表示 3D 形状，而是用 vector set（一维 latent token 序列）来表示 3D shape 的 implicit function。Latent 由 Hunyuan3D-ShapeVAE 得到，它沿用 Michelangelo 的 variational encoder-decoder transformer 结构，输入是 polygon mesh。段落没有明确写出用的是 SDF 还是 occupancy，这一点还需要进一步确认。
2e0d0789-p004-c01 · PDF p. 4 · Original passage
This design is driven by the successful applications of latent diffusion models in image and video generation. Specifically, our shape generation model consists of (1) an autoencoder – Hunyuan3D-ShapeV AE (Sec. 3.1) that compresses the shape of a 3D asset represented by polygon mesh into a sequence of continuous tokens in the latent space; (2) a flow-based diffusion model – Hunyuan3D-DiT (Sec. 3.2), trained on the latent space of ShapeV AE for predicting object token sequences from a user-provided image. The predicted tokens are further decoded into a polygon mesh with V AE decoder. The details of these models are illustrated below. 3.1 Hunyuan3D-ShapeV AE Hunyuan3D-ShapeV AE employs vector sets, a compact neural representation for 3D shapes proposed by 3DShape2VecSet [112], which has also been leveraged in the recent work Dora [11]. Followed by Michelangelo [119], we use a variational encoder-decoder transformer for shape compression and decoding.
2e0d0789-p018-c01 · PDF p. 18 · Original passage
As a result, neural representations of 3D shapes have emerged as a new research focus, aiming to enhance efficiency. Pioneering methods such as 3DShape2VecSet [112], Michelangelo [119], CLAY [114], and Dora [11] represent 3D shapes using vector sets (one-dimensional latent token sequences proposed by 3DShape2VecSet [112]), significantly improving representation efficiency. Another approach involves structured representations (e.g., triplane [68, 7, 26] or sparse volume [63, 120, 72]) to encode 3D shapes, which better preserve spatial priors but are less efficient than vector sets. Inspired by recent advances in Latent Diffusion Models, Hunyuan3D 2.0 employs vector sets to represent 3D shapes’ implicit functions, alleviating the compression and fitting demands on neural networks and achieving a breakthrough in shape generation performance. Shape Generative models.The evolution of generative model paradigms has continually influenced shape generation research.
From the paper
论文中的 shape 表示与编码方式
论文说 ShapeVAE 把 polygon mesh 表示的 3D 资产压缩成 latent 空间中的一串连续 token。它采用 3DShape2VecSet 提出的 vector set 作为紧凑的神经表示，并遵循 Michelangelo 使用 variational encoder-decoder transformer 做压缩和解码。
2e0d0789-p004-c01 · PDF p. 4 · Original passage
This design is driven by the successful applications of latent diffusion models in image and video generation. Specifically, our shape generation model consists of (1) an autoencoder – Hunyuan3D-ShapeV AE (Sec. 3.1) that compresses the shape of a 3D asset represented by polygon mesh into a sequence of continuous tokens in the latent space; (2) a flow-based diffusion model – Hunyuan3D-DiT (Sec. 3.2), trained on the latent space of ShapeV AE for predicting object token sequences from a user-provided image. The predicted tokens are further decoded into a polygon mesh with V AE decoder. The details of these models are illustrated below. 3.1 Hunyuan3D-ShapeV AE Hunyuan3D-ShapeV AE employs vector sets, a compact neural representation for 3D shapes proposed by 3DShape2VecSet [112], which has also been leveraged in the recent work Dora [11]. Followed by Michelangelo [119], we use a variational encoder-decoder transformer for shape compression and decoding.
Explanation
implicit function 与 vector set 的关系
可以这样理解：论文把形状看作一个隐式函数（给定空间中的查询点，输出该点的占据或场值），vector set 是存放这个函数信息的一组 latent 向量。解码时先用 grid queries 在空间网格上查询，再通过 Marching Cube 提取出 mesh。这是我根据段落和图示做的解释，段落本身没有逐步描述解码过程。
2e0d0789-p018-c01 · PDF p. 18 · Original passage
As a result, neural representations of 3D shapes have emerged as a new research focus, aiming to enhance efficiency. Pioneering methods such as 3DShape2VecSet [112], Michelangelo [119], CLAY [114], and Dora [11] represent 3D shapes using vector sets (one-dimensional latent token sequences proposed by 3DShape2VecSet [112]), significantly improving representation efficiency. Another approach involves structured representations (e.g., triplane [68, 7, 26] or sparse volume [63, 120, 72]) to encode 3D shapes, which better preserve spatial priors but are less efficient than vector sets. Inspired by recent advances in Latent Diffusion Models, Hunyuan3D 2.0 employs vector sets to represent 3D shapes’ implicit functions, alleviating the compression and fitting demands on neural networks and achieving a breakthrough in shape generation performance. Shape Generative models.The evolution of generative model paradigms has continually influenced shape generation research.
2e0d0789-p005-fig3 · PDF p. 5 · Figure: PaperLens description + authors' caption
Figure 3 (PaperLens description of the image): Figure 3 is a architecture diagram of the Hunyuan3D-ShapeVAE encoder and decoder. On the left, two small point-cloud renderings labeled Uniform and Importance feed into FPS blocks that produce point queries, followed by a Cross Attention block and a stack of Self Attention blocks marked x 8, with the output combined into Latent Tokens shown as a row of small colored squares. On the right, grid queries are shown as a vertical column of colored bars labeled Grid Queries, passed through a Cross Attention block and a Self Attention block marked x 16, then a Marching Cube step produces a Decoded Mesh rendered as a 3D shape. Arrows indicate the flow of data and the KV and Q connections between blocks. Not readable: Small labels such as the tensor dimensions and the FPS and Q/KV tags on the arrows are tiny and only partly legible; the point-cloud renderings are too small to read fine detail. Instead of only using uniform sampling on mesh surface, We have developed an importance sampling strategy to extract high-frequency detail information from the input mesh surface, such as edges and corners. From the paper
与其他表示方式的对比及采样策略
论文指出 triplane 或 sparse volume 等结构化表示更好地保留了空间先验，但效率低于 vector set。在编码端，论文没有只用均匀采样，而是加入 importance sampling 来抓取边和角等高频细节。论文同时提到 point query 配合 importance sampling 是其 encoder 的做法。
2e0d0789-p018-c01 · PDF p. 18 · Original passage
As a result, neural representations of 3D shapes have emerged as a new research focus, aiming to enhance efficiency. Pioneering methods such as 3DShape2VecSet [112], Michelangelo [119], CLAY [114], and Dora [11] represent 3D shapes using vector sets (one-dimensional latent token sequences proposed by 3DShape2VecSet [112]), significantly improving representation efficiency. Another approach involves structured representations (e.g., triplane [68, 7, 26] or sparse volume [63, 120, 72]) to encode 3D shapes, which better preserve spatial priors but are less efficient than vector sets. Inspired by recent advances in Latent Diffusion Models, Hunyuan3D 2.0 employs vector sets to represent 3D shapes’ implicit functions, alleviating the compression and fitting demands on neural networks and achieving a breakthrough in shape generation performance. Shape Generative models.The evolution of generative model paradigms has continually influenced shape generation research.
2e0d0789-p010-c01 · PDF p. 10 · Original passage
5.1 3D Shape Generation Shape generation is crucial for 3D generation, as high-fidelity and high-resolution bare meshes form the foundation for downstream tasks. In this section, we compare and evaluate the capability of 3D shape generation in Hunyuan3D 2.0 from two perspectives: shape reconstruction and shape generation. Baselines.We compare the reconstruction performance of Hunyuan3D-ShapeV AE with 3DShape2VecSet [112], Michelangelo [ 119], and Direct3D [ 99]. The mentioned methods represent the state-of-the-art ShapeV AE architecture, and the core differences are neural representations, where 3DShape2VecSet uses a downsampled vector set, point query; Michelangelo utilizes a learnable vector set, learnable query; Direct3D leverages learnable triplane; Hunyuan3D-ShapeV AE employees point query with importance sampling. Note that, except Direct3D, which requires a 3072 token length (suffering significant performance degeneration when reducing token length), all V AE models compare by 1024 token length. Hunyuan3D-DiT is compared with several state-of-the-art baselines. Open-source baselines are Michelangelo [119], Craftsman 1.5 [49], and Trellis [101].

On the right, grid queries are shown as a vertical column of colored bars labeled Grid Queries, passed through a Cross Attention block and a Self Attention block marked x 16, then a Marching Cube step produces a Decoded Mesh rendered as a 3D shape. PaperLens reply (expand a source to see the cited text; highlighted = evidence quote that occurs in it)
Short answer
论文在纹理生成前设置了一个专门的图像去光照（delighting）预处理步骤，目的是防止输入图的光照和阴影被烘焙进纹理贴图。因此，纹理流程的设计默认输入会先经过 delight 处理，但检索到的段落没有说明它对真实网络照片（复杂光照、遮挡）的效果如何。
2e0d0789-p007-c01 · PDF p. 7 · Original passage
To achieve these objectives, we employ a three-stage framework, including a pre-processing stage (Sec. 4.1), a multi-view image synthesis stage (Sec. 4.2, Hunyuan3D-Paint), and a texture baking stage based on dense multi-view inference (Sec. 4.3). Other details related to model training and textand image-to-texture pipeline are provided in Sec. 4.4. Fig. 5 illustrates the complete pipeline of our texture map synthesis method. 4.1 Pre-processing Image Delighting Module.The reference image typically exhibits pronounced and varied illumination and shadow, whether collected by the user or generated by T2I models. Directly inputting such images into the multi-view generation framework can cause illumination and shadows to be baked into the texture maps. To address this issue, we leverage a delighting procedure on the input image via an image-to-image approach [6] before multi-view generation. Specifically, to train such an image delighting model, we collect a large-scale 3D dataset and render it under the illumination of a random HDRI environmental map and an even white light to form the corresponding pair-wise image data.
2e0d0789-p009-c04 · PDF p. 9 · Original passage
Multi-view images are rendered under the illumination of an even white light to accommodate our delighting model. Specifically, we render the reference image with a random azimuth and a fixed range of elevation from -20 to 20 degrees. This variation disrupts the consistency between the reference and generated images, thereby increasing the robustness of our texture generation framework. We directly train on 512 × 512 resolution with a total of 80,000 steps, a batch size of 48, and a learning rate of 5×10 −5. We use 1000 warm-up steps and the "trailing" scheduler proposed by ZSNR. General Text- and Image-to-Texture.It is worth noting that Hunyuan3D-Paint not only generates high-quality texture maps for generated meshes but also supports arbitrary texture generation guided by any text or image input provided by the user for any geometric model. To achieve this, we leverage
From the paper
论文中的去光照预处理模块
论文说明，参考图像通常带有明显且多变的光照和阴影，如果直接送入多视角生成框架，光照和阴影会被烘焙进纹理。作者因此在多视角生成之前，用图像到图像的方法对输入图做 delight 处理。训练数据是把大规模 3D 数据集在随机 HDRI 环境光和均匀白光下渲染成成对图像。

纹理训练时的白光渲染设定
论文提到，多视角图像是在均匀白光下渲染的，以配合其 delighting 模型。参考图像使用随机方位角和 -20 到 20 度的俯仰角范围渲染，作者认为这种变化能打破参考图与生成图之间的一致性，从而提升纹理生成框架的鲁棒性。

Explanation
对 delight 假设的理解
可以把 delight 理解为先把输入照片还原成更接近“均匀照明下的外观”，去掉高光和投影，再让纹理模型去生成颜色。这样生成的贴图更接近物体本身的颜色，而不是拍摄时的光照。从段落看，这是纹理流程中的一个前置阶段，而不是可选的后处理。
2e0d0789-p007-c01 · PDF p. 7 · Original passage
To achieve these objectives, we employ a three-stage framework, including a pre-processing stage (Sec. 4.1), a multi-view image synthesis stage (Sec. 4.2, Hunyuan3D-Paint), and a texture baking stage based on dense multi-view inference (Sec. 4.3). Other details related to model training and textand image-to-texture pipeline are provided in Sec. 4.4. Fig. 5 illustrates the complete pipeline of our texture map synthesis method. 4.1 Pre-processing Image Delighting Module.The reference image typically exhibits pronounced and varied illumination and shadow, whether collected by the user or generated by T2I models. Directly inputting such images into the multi-view generation framework can cause illumination and shadows to be baked into the texture maps. To address this issue, we leverage a delighting procedure on the input image via an image-to-image approach [6] before multi-view generation. Specifically, to train such an image delighting model, we collect a large-scale 3D dataset and render it under the illumination of a random HDRI environmental map and an even white light to form the corresponding pair-wise image data.

如果你的 AR 应用需要在虚拟场景中用自己的光照重新渲染资产，那么烘焙了原始光照的纹理会造成冲突，去光照这一步的思路可能有参考价值。不过这只是基于论文思路的推断，最终是否采用由你决定，并需要结合你的数据和评估标准。

论文在纹理生成前设置了一个专门的图像去光照（delighting）预处理步骤，目的是防止输入图的光照和阴影被烘焙进纹理贴图。因此，纹理流程的设计默认输入会先经过 delight 处理，但检索到的段落没有说明它对真实网络照片（复杂光照、遮挡）的效果如何。
  • [From the paper] 论文中的去光照预处理模块: 论文说明，参考图像通常带有明显且多变的光照和阴影，如果直接送入多视角生成框架，光照和阴影会被烘焙进纹理。作者因此在多视角生成之前，用图像到图像的方法对输入图做 delight 处理。训练数据是把大规模 3D 数据集在随机 HDRI 环境光和均匀白光下渲染成成对图像。
  • [From the paper] 纹理训练时的白光渲染设定: 论文提到，多视角图像是在均匀白光下渲染的，以配合其 delighting 模型。参考图像使用随机方位角和 -20 到 20 度的俯仰角范围渲染，作者认为这种变化能打破参考图与生成图之间的一致性，从而提升纹理生成框架的鲁棒性。
  • [Explanation] 对 delight 假设的理解: 可以把 delight 理解为先把输入照片还原成更接近“均匀照明下的外观”，去掉高光和投影，再让纹理模型去生成颜色。这样生成的贴图更接近物体本身的颜色，而不是拍摄时的光照。从段落看，这是纹理流程中的一个前置阶段，而不是可选的后处理。
  • [For your research] 对你 AR 研究的可能启示: 如果你的 AR 应用需要在虚拟场景中用自己的光照重新渲染资产，那么烘焙了原始光照的纹理会造成冲突，去光照这一步的思路可能有参考价值。不过这只是基于论文思路的推断，最终是否采用由你决定，并需要结合你的数据和评估标准。

PaperLens reply (expand a source to see the cited text; highlighted = evidence quote that occurs in it)
Short answer
论文明确写的是：如果把带光照和阴影的参考图直接输入多视角生成框架，光照和阴影“可能”被烘焙进纹理贴图，因此作者在前面加了去光照模块。“光照被烘焙进纹理”是论文的原话，但它写的是“can cause”（可能导致），并非已经用实验证明的结果。论文在检索到的段落中没有给出不做 delight 时具体的失败案例或对比实验，所以具体会出什么问题，只能算我的推断。
2e0d0789-p007-c01 · PDF p. 7 · Original passage
To achieve these objectives, we employ a three-stage framework, including a pre-processing stage (Sec. 4.1), a multi-view image synthesis stage (Sec. 4.2, Hunyuan3D-Paint), and a texture baking stage based on dense multi-view inference (Sec. 4.3). Other details related to model training and textand image-to-texture pipeline are provided in Sec. 4.4. Fig. 5 illustrates the complete pipeline of our texture map synthesis method. 4.1 Pre-processing Image Delighting Module.The reference image typically exhibits pronounced and varied illumination and shadow, whether collected by the user or generated by T2I models. Directly inputting such images into the multi-view generation framework can cause illumination and shadows to be baked into the texture maps. To address this issue, we leverage a delighting procedure on the input image via an image-to-image approach [6] before multi-view generation. Specifically, to train such an image delighting model, we collect a large-scale 3D dataset and render it under the illumination of a random HDRI environmental map and an even white light to form the corresponding pair-wise image data.
From the paper
论文对未去光照的说法
作者指出，参考图通常带有明显且多变的光照和阴影，无论是用户拍摄的还是 T2I 模型生成的；直接把这类图输入多视角生成框架，可能导致光照和阴影被烘焙进纹理贴图。为此，他们在多视角生成前用图像到图像的方法对输入图做去光照处理。

去光照模型的训练方式

2e0d0789-p009-c04 · PDF p. 9 · Original passage
Multi-view images are rendered under the illumination of an even white light to accommodate our delighting model. Specifically, we render the reference image with a random azimuth and a fixed range of elevation from -20 to 20 degrees. This variation disrupts the consistency between the reference and generated images, thereby increasing the robustness of our texture generation framework. We directly train on 512 × 512 resolution with a total of 80,000 steps, a batch size of 48, and a learning rate of 5×10 −5. We use 1000 warm-up steps and the "trailing" scheduler proposed by ZSNR. General Text- and Image-to-Texture.It is worth noting that Hunyuan3D-Paint not only generates high-quality texture maps for generated meshes but also supports arbitrary texture generation guided by any text or image input provided by the user for any geometric model. 不做 delight 时可能的具体问题（推断）
这是我的推断而非论文结论：如果输入图里已有阴影和高光，多视角模型可能把这些明暗当作物体本身的颜色来学习，于是生成的各视角纹理在光照方向上不一致，烘焙后的贴图也带着原图的阴影。论文检索到的段落没有展示这种现象的对比结果。

For your research
对你 AR 研究的启示与限制
对 AR 来说，去光照得到的近似“无光照”纹理更容易在目标环境中重新打光，这与你的研究目标相关。但论文的去光照模型是在渲染的 3D 数据上训练的，检索到的段落没有说明它对真实网络照片（复杂光照、遮挡、镜面反射）的泛化效果，这一点需要你自己用真实照片验证。

我的推测（非论文结论）
我推测，由于去光照模型的训练对数据是合成渲染，真实网络照片的光照分布和成像条件可能与训练数据有差距，重阴影区域的去光照效果可能不稳定。对遮挡的情况，单张图的去光照只能处理可见像素，被遮挡区域的信息缺失可能需要多视角模型去补全，这一步论文段落中没有说明其效果。

For your research
对你 AR 研究的可能用途

PaperLens reply (expand a source to see the cited text; highlighted = evidence quote that occurs in it)
Short answer
从检索到的段落看，论文用了三类评估：形状重建用 V-IoU 和 S-IoU；形状与纹理生成用 ULIP、Uni3D 等相似度指标；带纹理的 3D 资产用渲染图的 FID_CLIP、CLIP-score、CMMD、LPIPS，另外还有用户研究。但目前检索到的段落没有说明在真实网络照片上做过验证，所以现有证据不足以确认这一点。
2e0d0789-p010-c02 · PDF p. 10 · Original passage
Hunyuan3D-DiT is compared with several state-of-the-art baselines. Open-source baselines are Michelangelo [119], Craftsman 1.5 [49], and Trellis [101]. Closed-source baselines are Shape Model 1, Shape Model 2, and Shape Model 3. Metrics.We employ the Intersection of Union (IoU) to measure the reconstruction performance. Specifically, we compute randomly sampled volume points IoU (V-IoU) and near-surface region IoU (S-IoU) to reflect the reconstruction performance comprehensively. To evaluate shape generative performance, we employ ULIP [ 105] and Uni3D [ 122] to compute the similarity between the generated mesh and input images (ULIP-I and Uni3D-I) and the similarity between the generated mesh and images prompt synthesizing by the vision language model [13] (ULIP-T and Uni3D-T). Shape Reconstruction Comparisons.The Numerical comparison of shape reconstruction is shown in Tab. 1. According to the table, Hunyuan3D-ShapeV AE overwhelms all baselines. Comparisons among Hunyuan3D-ShapeV AE, 3DShape2VecSet, and Michelangelo demonstrate the effectiveness of the importance sampling strategies. Fig.
2e0d0789-p013-c03 · PDF p. 13 · Original passage
Metrics.We mainly measure the generative quality of textured 3D assets by their renderings. Similar to Sec. 5.2, we employ F IDCLIP to compute the image content distance, CLIP-score to reflect semantic alignment, CMMD to measure the similarity in the image details, and LPIPS to evaluate the consistency between rendering from generated textured 3D assets and given image prompts. Comparisons.The numerical results reported in the Tab. 4 indicate that Hunyuan3D 2.0 surpasses all baselines in the quality of generated textured 3D assets and the condition following ability. The illustration in the Fig. 11 demonstrates that Hunyuan3D 2.0 produces the textured 3D assets with the highest quality. Even for the text in the image prompt, our model can produce the correct bumps on the shape surface and an accurate texture map according to the geometric conditions. The rest of the cases demonstrate the ability of our model to generate high-resolution and high-fidelity results with complex actions or scenes.
2e0d0789-p015-c01 · PDF p. 15 · Original passage
The user study results in Fig. 10 indicate that Hunyuan3D 2.0 outperforms comparative methods, particularly in its ability to adhere to image conditions. From the paper
形状与纹理的主要指标
论文先用体积 IoU（V-IoU）和近表面 IoU（S-IoU）衡量重建质量；对生成形状，则用 ULIP 和 Uni3D 计算生成网格与输入图像（ULIP-I、Uni3D-I）以及与视觉语言模型合成的提示图像（ULIP-T、Uni3D-T）之间的相似度。对带纹理的 3D 资产，主要通过渲染结果评估，使用 FID_CLIP、CLIP-score、CMMD 和 LPIPS，其中 LPIPS 用于衡量渲染结果与给定图像提示的一致性。

2e0d0789-p013-c03 · PDF p. 13 · Original passage
Metrics.We mainly measure the generative quality of textured 3D assets by their renderings. Similar to Sec. 5.2, we employ F IDCLIP to compute the image content distance, CLIP-score to reflect semantic alignment, CMMD to measure the similarity in the image details, and LPIPS to evaluate the consistency between rendering from generated textured 3D assets and given image prompts. Comparisons.The numerical results reported in the Tab. 4 indicate that Hunyuan3D 2.0 surpasses all baselines in the quality of generated textured 3D assets and the condition following ability. The illustration in the Fig. 11 demonstrates that Hunyuan3D 2.0 produces the textured 3D assets with the highest quality. Even for the text in the image prompt, our model can produce the correct bumps on the shape surface and an accurate texture map according to the geometric conditions. The rest of the cases demonstrate the ability of our model to generate high-resolution and high-fidelity results with complex actions or scenes.

Closed-source baselines are Shape Model 1, Shape Model 2, and Shape Model 3. Metrics.We employ the Intersection of Union (IoU) to measure the reconstruction performance. Specifically, we compute randomly sampled volume points IoU (V-IoU) and near-surface region IoU (S-IoU) to reflect the reconstruction performance comprehensively. To evaluate shape generative performance, we employ ULIP [ 105] and Uni3D [ 122] to compute the similarity between the generated mesh and input images (ULIP-I and Uni3D-I) and the similarity between the generated mesh and images prompt synthesizing by the vision language model [13] (ULIP-T and Uni3D-T). Limitation / uncertainty
指标定义前后不一致（需核实原文）
两段文字对 LPIPS 的描述不同：一处说它衡量与真值图（ground-truth）的一致性，另一处说它衡量与给定图像提示（image prompts）的一致性。这一不一致可能影响对结果的解读，建议回到原文和附录核对。
2e0d0789-p013-c01 · PDF p. 13 · Original passage
Metrics.We apply several frequently used image-level metrics to enable a fair comparison of texture map generation. Specifically, we leverage a CLIP-version of Fréchet Inception Distance F IDCLIP to compute the distance between the rendering of the generated textured map in semantic perspectives. We use the implementation of Clean-FID [66]. Besides, the recently introduced CLIP Maximum-Mean Discrepancy (CMMD) [38] is utilized to serve as another important criterion, which is a more accurate measurement of images with rich details. In addition to these two metrics, we also use CLIP-score [71] to validate semantic alignment between renderings of the generated texture map and given prompt and LPIPS [117] to estimate the consistency between renderings of the generated texture map and ground-truth images. Comparisons.The numerical comparison of text-to-texturing is shown in Tab. 3, showcasing that Hunyuan3D-Paint achieves the best generative quality and semantic following. The visual comparison refers to Fig. 8. The fish and rabbit show that our model produces the most condition-following results.
2e0d0789-p013-c03 · PDF p. 13 · Original passage
Metrics.We mainly measure the generative quality of textured 3D assets by their renderings. Similar to Sec. 5.2, we employ F IDCLIP to compute the image content distance, CLIP-score to reflect semantic alignment, CMMD to measure the similarity in the image details, and LPIPS to evaluate the consistency between rendering from generated textured 3D assets and given image prompts. Comparisons.The numerical results reported in the Tab. 4 indicate that Hunyuan3D 2.0 surpasses all baselines in the quality of generated textured 3D assets and the condition following ability. The illustration in the Fig. 11 demonstrates that Hunyuan3D 2.0 produces the textured 3D assets with the highest quality. Even for the text in the image prompt, our model can produce the correct bumps on the shape surface and an accurate texture map according to the geometric conditions. The rest of the cases demonstrate the ability of our model to generate high-resolution and high-fidelity results with complex actions or scenes.
PaperLens inference
AR 适用性：这是我的推断
检索到的段落只评估渲染图的生成质量、语义对齐和与图像条件的一致性，没有 AR 场景相关的评估，例如在真实环境中的光照一致性或实时性。因此「适合 AR 场景」只能视为我的推断，不能当作论文结论。

C13

Paper: Game Studies - Defining Game Mechanics
Reading goal: Get a general overview of this paper

Turn 1

For A · previous PaperLens reply
(first message of the conversation)
Learner message
你好，我想先了解一下這篇論文大概在講什麼？
Move the simulated learner was given
Ask in a broad way what this article is about.
PaperLens reply (expand a source to see the cited text; highlighted = evidence quote that occurs in it)
Short answer
這篇論文由 Miguel Sicart 撰寫，目的是為「遊戲機制（game mechanics）」提出一個更精確的形式化定義。作者借用物件導向程式設計的概念，把遊戲機制定義為由「代理者（agents）」調用、用來與遊戲狀態互動的「方法（methods）」，並希望藉此連結形式分析、控制器設計與使用者體驗。
ov-1 · PDF p. 1-1 · PaperLens-generated overview summary (not the authors' words)
The article by Miguel Sicart addresses the ambiguity of the term 'game mechanics' in game research and design, using the cover system in Gears of War and the minimalist game Orbital as opening examples. It proposes a formal definition that treats game mechanics, drawing on object-oriented programming concepts, as methods invoked by agents for interacting with the game state, defined in relation to rules, challenges and emotions used in an instrumental way. The introduction states the aims: to provide an analytical tool for describing game mechanics, to connect formal analysis with controller design and user experience, and to set up a comparative analysis of Shadow of the Colossus, Rez and Every Extend Extra. The pages also outline a plan that includes a review of previous definitions of mechanics, a discussion of primary and secondary mechanics, and a conclusion on the definition's shortcomings, though these later sections are not included in the text shown.
f17ba500-p001-c04 · PDF p. 1 · Original passage
In this article, I propose a definition of game mechanics useful for the analysis of games and their formal constituents. This definition will allow for formalized analysis of game structures, and it will also open up for the possibility of connecting formal game analysis with research on controller designs and user experience. I define game mechanics, using concepts from object-oriented programming, as methods invoked by agents, designed for interaction with the game state. With this formalized definition, I intend to: Provide a tool to discover, describe, and interrelate game mechanics in any given game. Define mechanics also in relation to elements of the game system, game hardware and player experience, mapping mechanics to input procedures and player emotions. Even though I will be mentioning concepts like game rules, challenges, emotions and user experience, it is not my intention to enter the debate on those topics. Here, I use those concepts in a relational way: defining game mechanics requires mentioning and acknowledging rules, challenges and emotions.
From the paper
論文要解決的問題
作者指出「遊戲機制」一詞在遊戲研究與設計中定義含糊，並以 Gears of War 的掩體機制和極簡遊戲 Orbital 作為開場例子。作者希望提供一個分析工具，用來描述、發現並彼此關聯遊戲中的機制。
ov-1 · PDF p. 1-1 · PaperLens-generated overview summary (not the authors' words)
The article by Miguel Sicart addresses the ambiguity of the term 'game mechanics' in game research and design, using the cover system in Gears of War and the minimalist game Orbital as opening examples. It proposes a formal definition that treats game mechanics, drawing on object-oriented programming concepts, as methods invoked by agents for interacting with the game state, defined in relation to rules, challenges and emotions used in an instrumental way. The introduction states the aims: to provide an analytical tool for describing game mechanics, to connect formal analysis with controller design and user experience, and to set up a comparative analysis of Shadow of the Colossus, Rez and Every Extend Extra. The pages also outline a plan that includes a review of previous definitions of mechanics, a discussion of primary and secondary mechanics, and a conclusion on the definition's shortcomings, though these later sections are not included in the text shown.
f17ba500-p001-c04 · PDF p. 1 · Original passage
In this article, I propose a definition of game mechanics useful for the analysis of games and their formal constituents. This definition will allow for formalized analysis of game structures, and it will also open up for the possibility of connecting formal game analysis with research on controller designs and user experience. I define game mechanics, using concepts from object-oriented programming, as methods invoked by agents, designed for interaction with the game state. With this formalized definition, I intend to: Provide a tool to discover, describe, and interrelate game mechanics in any given game. Define mechanics also in relation to elements of the game system, game hardware and player experience, mapping mechanics to input procedures and player emotions. Even though I will be mentioning concepts like game rules, challenges, emotions and user experience, it is not my intention to enter the debate on those topics. Here, I use those concepts in a relational way: defining game mechanics requires mentioning and acknowledging rules, challenges and emotions.
From the paper
核心定義與研究架構
作者將遊戲機制定義為「由代理者調用、為與遊戲狀態互動而設計的方法」，並說明這個定義比以往方法更少以人類為中心，因為人類玩家、AI 或機器人都可以調用機制。論文也區分規則與機制：規則提供可能性空間並規範狀態轉換，機制則是與遊戲狀態互動的方法。
ov-3 · PDF p. 3-3 · PaperLens-generated overview summary (not the authors' words)
These pages define game mechanics as methods invoked by agents, designed for interaction with the game state, borrowing terminology from object-oriented programming. The author argues this definition is less anthropocentric than prior approaches, since mechanics can be invoked by human players, AI agents, or bots, with examples drawn from Shadow of the Colossus and Gears of War. The text also argues that the framework supports mapping mechanics to input devices, using UML-style modeling, and discusses how fighting-game mastery relates to input mapping. Finally, it introduces 'context mechanics' triggered by the player's situation in the game world, illustrated with Gears of War, Assassin's Creed and Wario Ware, Inc.; the author notes that studying bots' access to mechanics is outside the article's scope.
ov-4 · PDF p. 4-4 · PaperLens-generated overview summary (not the authors' words)
These pages from Sicart's article 'Defining Game Mechanics' argue for an ontological distinction between rules and mechanics: rules provide the possibility space and regulate state transitions, while mechanics are methods designed for interaction with the game state. The author illustrates this with Shadow of the Colossus, where climbing is a mechanic constrained by a stamina property derived from a rule, and the game loop evaluates interactions against those rules. The text also distinguishes designed gameplay from players' unpredictable uses, such as toyplay, noting that formal design cannot fully determine how a game is played. Finally, it begins an interlude on how qualifiers like core, primary and secondary mechanics fit the proposed definition, drawing on Salen and Zimmerman and Järvinen.
From the paper
文章結構與應用
論文先回顧既有的遊戲機制定義（如 Avedon、Järvinen、MDA 等），接著提出定義並討論核心機制與主要／次要機制，最後以 Shadow of the Colossus、Rez 與 Every Extend Extra 進行比較分析。作者在結論中承認這個定義仍有缺陷，並留下許多未深入探討的灰色地帶。
ov-2 · PDF p. 2-2 · PaperLens-generated overview summary (not the authors' words)
These pages review how game studies has analysed games through their formal elements, distinguishing systems, players, and player-system contexts. They trace the distinction between rules and mechanics from Avedon through Lundgren and Björk, Rouse, Fullerton et al., Cook, the MDA framework, and Järvinen. The authors argue that most prior definitions of game mechanics lack the precision needed for formal analysis, particularly in linking rules to mechanics and identifying individual mechanics. The pages also note that Järvinen's goal-oriented account struggles to explain sandbox play in games such as Sim City and Crackdown.
ov-5 · PDF p. 5-5 · PaperLens-generated overview summary (not the authors' words)
These pages argue that existing definitions of core mechanics, such as those of Salen and Zimmerman and Järvinen, lack precision because players repeat actions that are not core mechanics (e.g., jumping in FPS games). The author defines core mechanics systemically as game mechanics repeatedly used by agents to achieve a systemically rewarded end state, illustrated with Shadow of the Colossus, Orbital, Sim City and EverQuest. To handle complexity, the paper introduces primary and secondary mechanics, using Grand Theft Auto IV and Gears of War as examples, and notes these categories are analytical aids rather than rigid categories. The pages end by introducing compound game mechanics as sets of related mechanics functioning together within one delimited interaction mode, such as driving.

These pages come from a Game Studies article defining game mechanics, shifting from formal definition to application. The author compares Shadow of the Colossus, Rez and Every Extend Extra through their core input-based mechanics, arguing that each uses a tension-and-release structure. The stabbing mechanic in Shadow of the Colossus, the shoot mechanic in Rez and the explode mechanic in Every Extend Extra are read as design choices that shape intended player experiences such as powerlessness and epic achievement, or synaesthetic musical engagement. The section concludes that developers modified well-known core mechanics to alter player expectations and agency.
ov-7 · PDF p. 7-7 · PaperLens-generated overview summary (not the authors' words)
These pages conclude Sicart's article defining game mechanics as methods invoked by agents for interacting with the game world, drawing on object-oriented programming and game studies. They discuss practical uses of the definition: formally describing and modifying mechanics, improving game design documentation, and easing communication between designers and programmers. The conclusion argues that mechanics are performative while rules are normative, and that treating them as distinct is useful for analysing computer games. The author acknowledges that the definition is not the ultimate one, that many grey areas remain unexplored, and that no dominant definition of rules or mechanics exists in game studies. The pages end with acknowledgements and a reference list.

Short answer

f17ba500-p003-c00 · PDF p. 3 · Original passage
to player agency and game rules. In the following section I present a formal definition of game mechanics, together with the arguments that make it a more precise and inclusive approach than those reviewed in this section. Defining Game Mechanics Let's start with a definition: game mechanics are methods invoked by agents, designed for interaction with the game state. The different components of this definition require further explanation: "Methods invoked by agents" defines this approach to game mechanics, as it formalizes the use of terminology taken from the object oriented programming paradigm (Weisfeld, 2000). In this appropriation of the terminology, object orientation provides a set of metaphors that describe the elements of systems and their interrelations. I do not want to imply that the analysis of the source code of a game will reveal that all game mechanics have been implemented as methods of classes or that object-oriented programming should be considered a default methodology for the actual production of computer games. Nor am I implying that the Object Oriented Framework should be extended to a formal analysis of all elements of the game.

f17ba500-p001-c04 · PDF p. 1 · Original passage
In this article, I propose a definition of game mechanics useful for the analysis of games and their formal constituents. This definition will allow for formalized analysis of game structures, and it will also open up for the possibility of connecting formal game analysis with research on controller designs and user experience. I define game mechanics, using concepts from object-oriented programming, as methods invoked by agents, designed for interaction with the game state. With this formalized definition, I intend to: Provide a tool to discover, describe, and interrelate game mechanics in any given game. Define mechanics also in relation to elements of the game system, game hardware and player experience, mapping mechanics to input procedures and player emotions. Even though I will be mentioning concepts like game rules, challenges, emotions and user experience, it is not my intention to enter the debate on those topics. Here, I use those concepts in a relational way: defining game mechanics requires mentioning and acknowledging rules, challenges and emotions.
Explanation
用簡單的話理解「代理者」與「方法」
可以把代理者想成「做事的一方」，例如玩家操控的角色或遊戲中的 AI 機器人；把方法想成這個角色可以做的「動作」，例如跳躍、射擊。作者的例子是：在《Shadow of the Colossus》中，爬、騎馬、刺擊、跳躍、射箭等都是機制。
f17ba500-p003-c03 · PDF p. 3 · Original passage
For example, in Shadow of the Colossus we find the following mechanics: to climb, ride (the horse), stab, jump, shoot (arrows), whistle, grab, run (and variations like swim or dive). In Gears of War, a noncomprehensive list would be: cover, shoot, reload, throw (grenade), look (at a point of interest), use, give orders, switch weapons[2]. All of these are methods for agency within the game world, actions the player can take within the space of possibility created by the rules. This definition departs from the implicit anthropocentrism of previous approaches. Game mechanics can be invoked by any 2026/9/5 15:03 Game Studies - Defining Game Mechanics https://gamestudies.org/0802/articles/sicart 5/16 agent, be that human or part of the computer system. For instance, AI agents also have a number of methods available to interact with the gameworld. On occasion, those methods will be other than the ones made available to the human player, which can have consequences worth of analysis. This approach can be particularly interesting when trying to understand the effect of bots in MMORPGs, since bots are agents that optimize their interaction by focusing on a core set of mechanics.

f17ba500-p003-c04 · PDF p. 3 · Original passage
This approach can be particularly interesting when trying to understand the effect of bots in MMORPGs, since bots are agents that optimize their interaction by focusing on a core set of mechanics. This design choice may lead to an imbalance in the game system, in terms of its dynamics or its economy. Another extension of this approach would draw a distinction between agents in a game with mechanics and agents without access to mechanics. For example, some bots do have access to mechanics while other game agents do not have access to mechanics and hence cannot interact with the game state. This line of research, however, is outside the scope of this article. The second advantage is that it eases the mapping of mechanics to input devices, allowing for a great degree of granularity in the analysis of games. Applying the conceptual framework of Object Oriented programming determines that an agent invokes a mechanic in order to interact with the game[3]. When it comes to players, input devices - from mouse and keyboard to the Wii Fit Board - mediate this process. In Gears of War, the cover mechanic is invoked by pressing the A button in the controller.
From the paper
機制與規則的區別
作者認為規則提供可能性的空間並規範狀態之間的轉換，而機制則是實際與遊戲狀態互動的部分。作者的說法是：規則是為能動性而建模，機制是對能動性而建模。
f17ba500-p004-c00 · PDF p. 4 · Original passage
Implicit in this definition is an ontological difference between rules and mechanics. Game mechanics are concerned with the actual interaction with the game state, while rules provide the possibility space where that interaction is possible, regulating as well the transition between states. In this sense, rules are modeled after agency, while mechanics are modeled for agency. In this object oriented framework, rules could be considered general or particular properties of the game system and its agents. All objects in games have properties. These properties are often either rules or determined by rules. These rules are evaluated by a game loop, an algorithm that relates the current state of the game and the properties of the objects with a number of conditions that consequently can modify the game state. For example, the winning condition, the losing condition and the effects of action in the player's avatar health are calculated when running the game loop. This algorithm relates rules with mechanics, exemplifying the applicability of an ontological distinction between rules and mechanics.

f17ba500-p007-c03 · PDF p. 7 · Original passage
Perhaps the most significant is the ontological distinction between rules and mechanics. Game researchers have argued convincingly that mechanics could be understood as subsets of rules. However, rules are normative, while mechanics are performative, and I have argued that this ontological distinction can be extremely beneficial for the analysis of computer games. Game studies history shows that there is no dominant definition of key concepts like rules or mechanics, and that those that attempted have yet to succeed. This article should not be read as the ultimate definition of game mechanics. This definition is 2026/9/5 15:03 Game Studies - Defining Game Mechanics https://gamestudies.org/0802/articles/sicart 13/16 flawed, yet less so than some previous ones. My goal will be achieved if I have succeeded in communicating to the reader one simple notion: that it is possible and useful to understand game mechanics as different from game rules, and in that understanding, we can more clearly describe how games can be designed to affect players in unprecedented ways.
