These pages from the user study section of 'Why Johnny Can't Prompt' describe how ten non-expert participants iteratively designed prompts for a recipe chatbot (CarlaBot) using the BotDesigner tool, with interviewer advice given during the session and analysis via affinity diagrams and service blueprints. ov-13 · PDF p. 14-14 · PaperLens-generated overview summary (not the authors' words)
These pages discuss design implications and limitations from a study of end-user prompt design for LLM-backed chatbots (BotDesigner). They argue that users need more examples of an error class before feeling confident it is resolved, and that tools should make successful examples discoverable and support exploring cause-and-effect between prompt changes and conversational changes. The authors suggest designs that hide the instruction-like nature of prompting or have the app perform socially violating work itself, and note that seamful design may make the app's non-human nature visible. Stated limitations include a small, academic early-adopter sample, limited time for prompt design, and the need for future work on other populations, non-chatbot domains, and the role of social expectations (CASA).
Clarifying question
Which specific aspect of non-expert prompting does your study focus on (for example, how users test prompts, how they judge robustness, or how they handle the chatbot's role), so I can point to the most relevant findings?

PaperLens reply (expand a source to see the cited text; highlighted = evidence quote that occurs in it)
Short answer
Not quite. In this paper the participants are not held-out test data for measuring how well the LLM tool's prompts perform. They are non-AI-expert end users who tried to design prompts with a prototype tool (BotDesigner, a design probe), and the researchers observed and interviewed them about how they approached and struggled with that design work.

d606a330-p009-c00 · PDF p. 9 · Original passage
Why Johnny Can’t Prompt CHI ’23, April 23–28, 2023, Hamburg, Germany After this initial phase, again in the interest of observing subsequent behavior, we would ofer suggestions for prompts to try that were in line with the research around prompt design. Examples of this advice include “try reframing that ‘do not’ statement into a positive statement, say what it is you want the bot to say, not what you don’t want it to say” or “have you considered copying some of the Carla-Lizzie transcript directly into the preamble?” We did not intentionally vary this advice across participants; however, due to the unpredictable nature of which errors participants chose to focus on, not all participants received the same advice or advice in the same order, a limitation of our approach. Analysis & Evaluation. We asked participants to think aloud, then engaged in exploratory data analysis, transcribing all videos and observing what prompt design approaches participants attempted, and noting when participants appeared to struggle and where interviewers intervened.
Explanation
Why participants are not a test set
A held-out test set is data used to measure a model's accuracy or output quality. Here, the study measured how people behave when they write prompts: their strategies, their intuitions, and their breakdowns. The participants' prompting behaviour is the object of study, not a benchmark for the tool's outputs.

For your research
Possible relevance to your user study
For your project on how non-experts write prompts, the participants would be your study subjects whose behaviour and reasoning you analyse, rather than evaluation data for the LLM. You would need to decide what you measure, such as strategies, expectations, or outcomes, and how you will record them, for example with think-aloud protocols as in this paper. This is a suggestion for you to weigh against your own design.

d606a330-p007-c03 · PDF p. 7 · Original passage
Participants. All participants ( = 10) had little to no experience with prompt design; none had substantial prior experience 2Regression tests, in the context of software engineering and in the sense we intend here, are tests that verify that any new changes to a system do not reintroduce previously-resolved errors (called regressions). working with LLMs. We recruited participants with varying levels of programming and chatbot design experience. Recall that our focus here is understanding end-users in the domain of prompt design; experience in chatbot design was thus not considered grounds for exclusion. Our sample size was chosen in line with prior work around formative testing for usability [19, 43]: our goal is to explore the frst, “easy-to-fnd” problems users encounter when engaged in our task, and our experience with a pilot user suggested that problem discoverability was likely to be high. Our participant pool, skewed towards professionals and graduate students in STEM-related felds, is not representative of the population at large.

PaperLens reply (expand a source to see the cited text; highlighted = evidence quote that occurs in it)
Short answer
No. In this paper, the design probe is BotDesigner, a no-code prototype prompt-design tool that the authors built and gave to participants so they could observe how non-experts approach prompt design. It is not a baseline model that the study compares against, and the retrieved passages do not describe any such comparison.
d606a330-p002-c01 · PDF p. 2 · Original passage
Using this tool as a design probe, we observed how 10 participants without substantial prompt design experience executed a chatbot design task using BotDesigner, to explore a few diferent pieces of this larger question. Our fndings suggest that, while end-users can explore prompt designs opportunistically, they struggle to make robust, systematic progress. Their struggles echo many well-known struggles observed in end-user programming systems (EUPS) and non-expect users of interactive machine learning (iML) systems. Additional barriers to efective prompt design stem from limited conceptions of LLMs’ prompt understanding and execution abilities and their understandable inclinations to design prompts that resemble human-to-human instructions. We discuss these observations’ implications for designing efective end-user-facing LLM-based design tools, implications for education that improves LLM-andprompt literacy among programmers and the general public, and opportunities for further research. This paper makes three contributions.

We chose to create a design probe because the robustness of prompt strategies is highly dependent on specifc conversational contexts, as is users’ ability to create prompts and debug.
d606a330-p002-c01 · PDF p. 2 · Original passage
Using this tool as a design probe, we observed how 10 participants without substantial prompt design experience executed a chatbot design task using BotDesigner, to explore a few diferent pieces of this larger question. Our fndings suggest that, while end-users can explore prompt designs opportunistically, they struggle to make robust, systematic progress. Their struggles echo many well-known struggles observed in end-user programming systems (EUPS) and non-expect users of interactive machine learning (iML) systems. Additional barriers to efective prompt design stem from limited conceptions of LLMs’ prompt understanding and execution abilities and their understandable inclinations to design prompts that resemble human-to-human instructions. We discuss these observations’ implications for designing efective end-user-facing LLM-based design tools, implications for education that improves LLM-andprompt literacy among programmers and the general public, and opportunities for further research. This paper makes three contributions.
Explanation
Why a probe rather than a baseline
A design probe is used to provoke and observe behaviour, not to measure the performance of a reference system. The authors argue that hands-on design work reveals more than interviews about hypothetical scenarios, so the tool serves as a way to see how people think and act. Their stated goals were to let end-users create chatbots without code and evaluate their prompts, while observing their intuitive behaviour.
d606a330-p004-c01 · PDF p. 4 · Original passage
We chose to create a design probe because the robustness of prompt strategies is highly dependent on specifc conversational contexts, as is users’ ability to create prompts and debug. Enabling people to engage in designing LLM-and-prompt-based chatbots hands-on offers deeper insights than conducting interviews about hypothetical scenarios alone. We chose to purpose-build a prompt design tool because existing tools focus on enabling non-experts to experiment with various prompting strategies rather than crafting robust and generalizable prompts. 3.1 Designing a No-Code Prompt Design Tool as Probe Design Goals. We have two goals in designing BotDesigner: First, to enable end-users, without any prompt design experience or even programming or ML experience, to (i) create a chatbot using prompts, without code, and (ii) systematically evaluate their prompts. Second, to allow end-users to engage in this process in a fexible, open-ended way, allowing us to observe their intuitive behaviors and thought processes.
For your research
What this could mean for your user study
If you run a user study, a probe-style tool could be one way to elicit how non-experts write and debug prompts, rather than only asking them about it. The paper also presents BotDesigner as a contribution in itself, so you would need to decide whether you build a probe or use an existing tool, and justify that choice in your own design. This is a possible use you may want to weigh, not a conclusion the paper draws for your project.
d606a330-p002-c02 · PDF p. 2 · Original passage
This paper makes three contributions. First, it describes a novel, no-code LLM-and-prompt-based chatbot design tool that encourages iterative design and evaluation of robust prompt strategies (rather than opportunistic experimentation.) Second, it ofers a rare rich description of how non-experts intuitively approached prompt design, and where, how, and why they struggled. Finally, it identifes opportunities for non-expert-facing prompt design tools and open research questions in making LLM-powered design innovation accessible. 2 RELATED WORK 2.1 The Promises of Non-Expert Prompt Design Today’s chatbot design practice—i.e., designing multi-turn conversational interactions—follows a well-established workfow [12, 13, 24, 25, 39, 44]. Designers frst (i) identify the chatbot’s functionality or persona and draft ideal user-bot conversations, for example, through Wizard-of-Oz or having experts drafting scripts; (ii) create a dialogue fow template (e.g., “(1) greeting message; (2) questions to collect user intention; (3) ...

PaperLens reply (expand a source to see the cited text; highlighted = evidence quote that occurs in it)
Short answer
The study had 10 non-expert prompt designers. The passages describe the analysis as two authors comparing approaches across participants and categorizing struggles with affinity diagrams and a service blueprint, not as coding of think-aloud transcripts. The retrieved passages do not describe transcript coding, so that part of your question is not confirmed.
d606a330-p007-c02 · PDF p. 7 · Original passage
It is worth noting that although BotDesigner enables this full workfow, we do not necessarily expect all end-users to engage in all its steps. However, we did validate BotDesigner’s functionality and usability throughout this workfow via a set of pilot user studies (see Appendix A.3 for details) to ensure that these pieces are available should users choose to systematically assess their prompt designs. 3.3 User Study Design To understand how end users intuitively approach prompt design, we invited 10 non-expert prompt designers to use BotDesigner and think-aloud. We invited the participants to improve upon a baseline chatbot template towards given goals (see Tasks below), while we observed and took note of the various prompt design approaches and testing strategies they engaged in. We chose the task of improving a chatbot, instead of creating it from scratch, in order to avoid the Blank Page Syndrome, the efect where users staring at a blank page do not know where to begin. Participants.
d606a330-p009-c01 · PDF p. 9 · Original passage
Two of the authors then compared these approaches across participants and categorized their struggles using afnity diagrams [34] and by creating a service blueprint [5] that documents the specifc approaches individual participants attempt. We chose these methods from HCI and the feld of service design because our focus is on discovering opportunities created by a new technology, rather than understanding how an existing set of users engages in established work practices—a context in which a grounded theory approach would be more conventional. 4 USER STUDY FINDINGS We found the following: (1) Using BotDesigner, all participants were able to engage in ad hoc opportunistic iteration on prompts, with local testing of prompt changes. Two out of our ten participants required assistance in order to begin this process, but could then iterate on their own. (2) Participants’ struggles with generating prompts, evaluating prompt efectiveness, and explaining prompt efects, primarily stemmed from (a) over-generalization from single observations of success and failure of prompt changes; and (b) models of system behavior rooted in human-human interactions.
From the paper
Sample size as reported
The authors state that they invited 10 non-expert prompt designers to use BotDesigner and think-aloud, and they asked participants to improve a baseline chatbot template. The paper's findings also say that 2 of the 10 participants needed help to begin iterating on prompts.

PaperLens reply (expand a source to see the cited text; highlighted = evidence quote that occurs in it)
Short answer
Hunyuan3D 2.0 是一个两阶段的大规模 3D 生成系统：先由 Hunyuan3D-DiT 从单张输入图像生成裸网格（bare mesh），再由 Hunyuan3D-Paint 为该网格合成纹理图，并配套 Hunyuan3D-Studio 生产平台。对你的 AR 3D 资产研究而言，最相关的是「图像条件的形状生成」与「几何条件的纹理生成」这两个解耦的模块设计。
2e0d0789-p001-c00 · PDF p. 1 · Original passage
Hunyuan3D 2.0: Scaling Diffusion Models for High Resolution Textured 3D Assets Generation “Living out everyone’s imagination on creating and manipulating 3D assets.” —Hunyuan3D Team ∗ Abstract We present Hunyuan3D 2.0, an advanced large-scale 3D synthesis system for generating high-resolution textured 3D assets. This system includes two foundation components: a large-scale shape generation model – Hunyuan3D-DiT, and a largescale texture synthesis model – Hunyuan3D-Paint. The shape generative model, built on a scalable flow-based diffusion transformer, aims to create geometry that properly aligns with a given condition image, laying a solid foundation for downstream applications. The texture synthesis model, benefiting from strong geometric and diffusion priors, produces high-resolution and vibrant texture maps for either generated or hand-crafted meshes. Furthermore, we build Hunyuan3D-Studio – a versatile, user-friendly production platform that simplifies the re-creation process of 3D assets. It allows both professional and amateur users to manipulate or even animate their meshes efficiently.
2e0d0789-p003-c02 · PDF p. 3 · Original passage
To this end, we presentHunyuan3D 2.0, a 3D asset creation system with two strong open-sourced 3D foundation models:Hunyuan3D-DiTfor generative shape creation andHunyuan3D-Paintfor generative texture synthesis. Hunyuan3D 2.0 features a two-stage generation pipeline, starting with the creation of a bare mesh, followed by the synthesis of a texture map for that mesh. This strategy is effective for decoupling the difficulties of shape and texture generation [ 34, 107, 46, 47] and also provides flexibility for texturing either generated or handcrafted meshes. With this architecture, our shape creation model – Hunyuan3D-DiT, is designed as a large-scale flow-based diffusion model. As a prerequisite, we first train an autoencoder – Hunyuan3D-ShapeV AE using advanced techniques such as mesh surface importance sampling and variational token length to capture fine-grained details on the meshes. Then, we build up a dual-single stream transformer [ 45] on the latent space of our V AE with the flow-matching [53, 24] objective.
2e0d0789-p020-c00 · PDF p. 20 · Original passage
8 Conclusion In this report, we introduce an open-source 3D creation system—Hunyuan3D 2.0 —for generating textured meshes from images. We present Hunyuan3D-ShapeV AE, which is trained using a novel importance sampling method. This approach compresses each 3D object into a few latent tokens while minimizing reconstruction losses. Building on our V AE, we developed Hunyuan3D-DiT, an advanced diffusion transformer capable of generating visually appealing shapes that align precisely with input images. Besides, we introduce Hunyuan3D-Paint, another diffusion model designed to create textures for both our generated meshes and user-crafted meshes. With several innovative designs, our texture generation model, in conjunction with our shape generation model, can produce high-resolution, high-fidelity textured 3D assets from a single image. As we continue to make progress, we hope that Hunyuan3D 2.0 will serve as a robust baseline for large-scale 3D foundation models within the open-source community and facilitate future research endeavors.
From the paper
两阶段管线与主要组件
论文提出 Hunyuan3D 2.0 包含两个开源基础模型：用于形状生成的 Hunyuan3D-DiT 和用于纹理合成的 Hunyuan3D-Paint。作者称该管线先生成裸网格、再合成纹理图，这种设计解耦了形状与纹理的难度，并且既能给生成的网格也能给手工建模的网格贴图。

Hunyuan3D 2.0 features a two-stage generation pipeline, starting with the creation of a bare mesh, followed by the synthesis of a texture map for that mesh. From the paper
形状生成：ShapeVAE 与 Flow-based DiT
形状部分先训练自编码器 Hunyuan3D-ShapeVAE，使用网格表面重要性采样和可变 token 长度来捕捉细节；随后在其潜空间上构建双-单流 Transformer，并以 flow-matching 目标训练扩散模型。根据概览，ShapeVAE 通过在边和角附近多采样点来捕捉复杂细节，但这些细节来自概览摘要，原文细节需以论文正文为准。
2e0d0789-p003-c02 · PDF p. 3 · Original passage
To this end, we presentHunyuan3D 2.0, a 3D asset creation system with two strong open-sourced 3D foundation models:Hunyuan3D-DiTfor generative shape creation andHunyuan3D-Paintfor generative texture synthesis. Hunyuan3D 2.0 features a two-stage generation pipeline, starting with the creation of a bare mesh, followed by the synthesis of a texture map for that mesh. This strategy is effective for decoupling the difficulties of shape and texture generation [ 34, 107, 46, 47] and also provides flexibility for texturing either generated or handcrafted meshes. With this architecture, our shape creation model – Hunyuan3D-DiT, is designed as a large-scale flow-based diffusion model. Then, we build up a dual-single stream transformer [ 45] on the latent space of our V AE with the flow-matching [53, 24] objective.
2e0d0789-p020-c00 · PDF p. 20 · Original passage
8 Conclusion In this report, we introduce an open-source 3D creation system—Hunyuan3D 2.0 —for generating textured meshes from images. We present Hunyuan3D-ShapeV AE, which is trained using a novel importance sampling method. This approach compresses each 3D object into a few latent tokens while minimizing reconstruction losses. Building on our V AE, we developed Hunyuan3D-DiT, an advanced diffusion transformer capable of generating visually appealing shapes that align precisely with input images. Besides, we introduce Hunyuan3D-Paint, another diffusion model designed to create textures for both our generated meshes and user-crafted meshes. With several innovative designs, our texture generation model, in conjunction with our shape generation model, can produce high-resolution, high-fidelity textured 3D assets from a single image. As we continue to make progress, we hope that Hunyuan3D 2.0 will serve as a robust baseline for large-scale 3D foundation models within the open-source community and facilitate future research endeavors.
For your research
对你的 AR 研究的参考价值（research_use）
如果你的目标是从网络公开照片得到可用于 AR 的 3D 资产，可以借鉴其「先几何后纹理」的解耦思路，以及用图像条件（DINOv2 特征）约束形状对齐输入图像的做法。纹理部分的几何条件（法线图、坐标图）和多视角一致性机制也可能对你的纹理生成有参考意义。不过论文输入是干净的单张图像，且作者使用了自采数据集和大规模训练，你需要自行评估对真实网络照片（光照、遮挡、背景杂乱）的适用性。
2e0d0789-p003-c04 · PDF p. 3 · Original passage
The comparison shows the superiority of Hunyuan3D 2.0 in alignment between conditional images and generated meshes, generation of fine-grained details, and human preference ratings. 2 Hunyuan3D 2.0 Architecture In this section, we elaborate on the model architecture of Hunyuan3D 2.0, focusing on two main components: the shape generation model and the texture generation model. Fig. 2 illustrates the pipeline of Hunyuan3D 2.0 for creating a high-resolution textured 3D asset. Given an input image, Hunyuan3D-DiT initially generates a high-fidelity bare mesh via the shape generation model. This model comprises a Hunyuan3D-ShapeV AE and a Hunyuan3D-DiT, which will be discussed in Sec. 3. Subsequently, by leveraging strong geometric priors and the input image, we introduce Hunyuan3D- Paint as our texture generation model in Sec. 4. This model produces self-consistent multi-view outputs, which are used for baking high-definition texture maps.
2e0d0789-p020-c00 · PDF p. 20 · Original passage
8 Conclusion In this report, we introduce an open-source 3D creation system—Hunyuan3D 2.0 —for generating textured meshes from images. We present Hunyuan3D-ShapeV AE, which is trained using a novel importance sampling method. This approach compresses each 3D object into a few latent tokens while minimizing reconstruction losses. Limitation / uncertainty
需要注意的限制
目前提供的材料主要是概述和部分正文，尚未覆盖实验细节、AR 场景下的尺度与拓扑要求，以及对真实世界照片的鲁棒性评估。论文声称在用户研究中优于基线，但这些结论是作者的自评，需要结合原文的实验设置进一步核对。

For A · previous PaperLens reply
Hunyuan3D 2.0 是一个两阶段的大规模 3D 生成系统：先由 Hunyuan3D-DiT 从单张输入图像生成裸网格（bare mesh），再由 Hunyuan3D-Paint 为该网格合成纹理图，并配套 Hunyuan3D-Studio 生产平台。对你的 AR 3D 资产研究而言，最相关的是「图像条件的形状生成」与「几何条件的纹理生成」这两个解耦的模块设计。
  • [From the paper] 两阶段管线与主要组件: 论文提出 Hunyuan3D 2.0 包含两个开源基础模型：用于形状生成的 Hunyuan3D-DiT 和用于纹理合成的 Hunyuan3D-Paint。作者称该管线先生成裸网格、再合成纹理图，这种设计解耦了形状与纹理的难度，并且既能给生成的网格也能给手工建模的网格贴图。
  • [From the paper] 形状生成：ShapeVAE 与 Flow-based DiT: 形状部分先训练自编码器 Hunyuan3D-ShapeVAE，使用网格表面重要性采样和可变 token 长度来捕捉细节；随后在其潜空间上构建双-单流 Transformer，并以 flow-matching 目标训练扩散模型。根据概览，ShapeVAE 通过在边和角附近多采样点来捕捉复杂细节，但这些细节来自概览摘要，原文细节需以论文正文为准。
  • [For your research] 对你的 AR 研究的参考价值（research_use）: 如果你的目标是从网络公开照片得到可用于 AR 的 3D 资产，可以借鉴其「先几何后纹理」的解耦思路，以及用图像条件（DINOv2 特征）约束形状对齐输入图像的做法。纹理部分的几何条件（法线图、坐标图）和多视角一致性机制也可能对你的纹理生成有参考意义。不过论文输入是干净的单张图像，且作者使用了自采数据集和大规模训练，你需要自行评估对真实网络照片（光照、遮挡、背景杂乱）的适用性。
  • [Limitation / uncertainty] 需要注意的限制: 目前提供的材料主要是概述和部分正文，尚未覆盖实验细节、AR 场景下的尺度与拓扑要求，以及对真实世界照片的鲁棒性评估。论文声称在用户研究中优于基线，但这些结论是作者的自评，需要结合原文的实验设置进一步核对。

PaperLens reply (expand a source to see the cited text; highlighted = evidence quote that occurs in it)
Short answer
根据检索到的段落，Hunyuan3D-Paint 以第一阶段生成的裸网格为几何条件：它输入网格的法线图（normal maps）和位置图（position maps），结合参考图生成一组自洽的多视角图像，再把这些多视角图烘焙（baking）成高分辨率纹理贴图。不过，这些段落只给出了整体流程，没有详细说明网络结构、多视角一致性的具体机制以及烘焙和 UV 展开的细节。
2e0d0789-p004-c00 · PDF p. 4 · Original passage
Shape Generation Texture Synthesis Hunyuan3D-DiT Hunyuan3D-PaintHunyuan3D-ShapeVAE Image Delighting Reference Branch Generation Branch Multi-Task Attention Normal Position Cross Attention Self Attention Importance Uniform Cross Attention Self Attention x 8 Decoded Mesh FPS x 16 Q ··· ··· KV Latent Tokens ··· Marching Cube ··· ··· Grid Queries InputImage Delighted Image Single Image Super-Resolution Image Denoising Transformer Latent Tokens ··· Steps x N Baking Figure 2: An overall of Hunyuan3D 2.0 architecture for 3D generation. It consists of two main components: Hunyuan3D-DiT for generating bare mesh from a given input image and Hunyuan3D- Paint for generating a textured map for the generated bare mesh. Hunyuan3D-Paint takes geometry conditions – normal maps and position maps of generated mesh as inputs and generates multi-view images for texture baking. 3 Generative 3D Shape Generation Hunyuan3D 2.0 employs the architecture of the latent diffusion model [74, 112, 119, 114] for shape generation. This design is driven by the successful applications of latent diffusion models in image and video generation.
2e0d0789-p003-c03 · PDF p. 3 · Original passage
Then, we build up a dual-single stream transformer [ 45] on the latent space of our V AE with the flow-matching [53, 24] objective. Our texture generation model – Hunyuan3D-Paint is made of a novel mesh-conditioned multi-view generation pipeline and a number of sophisticated techniques for preprocessing and baking multi-view images into high-resolution texture maps. We performed an in-depth comparison ofHunyuan3D 2.0 in relation to leading 3D generation models worldwide, including three commercial closed-source end-to-end products, an end-to-end opensourced model Trellis [101], and several separate models [9, 37, 99, 111, 55, 59] for shape and texture generation. We report visual and quantitative evaluation results across three dimensions: generated textured mesh, bare mesh, and texture map. We also provided user study results on 300 test cases involving 50 participants. The comparison shows the superiority of Hunyuan3D 2.0 in alignment between conditional images and generated meshes, generation of fine-grained details, and human preference ratings.
2e0d0789-p003-c04 · PDF p. 3 · Original passage
The comparison shows the superiority of Hunyuan3D 2.0 in alignment between conditional images and generated meshes, generation of fine-grained details, and human preference ratings. 2 Hunyuan3D 2.0 Architecture In this section, we elaborate on the model architecture of Hunyuan3D 2.0, focusing on two main components: the shape generation model and the texture generation model. Fig. 2 illustrates the pipeline of Hunyuan3D 2.0 for creating a high-resolution textured 3D asset. Given an input image, Hunyuan3D-DiT initially generates a high-fidelity bare mesh via the shape generation model. This model comprises a Hunyuan3D-ShapeV AE and a Hunyuan3D-DiT, which will be discussed in Sec. 3. Subsequently, by leveraging strong geometric priors and the input image, we introduce Hunyuan3D- Paint as our texture generation model in Sec. 4. This model produces self-consistent multi-view outputs, which are used for baking high-definition texture maps.
From the paper
输入：来自网格的几何条件

Explanation
用通俗的话理解整个流程
可以把它理解成三步：先从第一阶段的网格渲染出法线图和位置图，告诉模型物体的表面朝向和空间位置；再以这些几何信息和参考图为条件，生成从多个视角看都应一致的图像；最后把这些多视角图投影回网格表面，合成一张纹理贴图。这是对论文流程的概括，不是论文原文。

The comparison shows the superiority of Hunyuan3D 2.0 in alignment between conditional images and generated meshes, generation of fine-grained details, and human preference ratings. 2 Hunyuan3D 2.0 Architecture In this section, we elaborate on the model architecture of Hunyuan3D 2.0, focusing on two main components: the shape generation model and the texture generation model. Fig. 2 illustrates the pipeline of Hunyuan3D 2.0 for creating a high-resolution textured 3D asset. Given an input image, Hunyuan3D-DiT initially generates a high-fidelity bare mesh via the shape generation model. This model comprises a Hunyuan3D-ShapeV AE and a Hunyuan3D-DiT, which will be discussed in Sec. 3. Subsequently, by leveraging strong geometric priors and the input image, we introduce Hunyuan3D- Paint as our texture generation model in Sec. 4. This model produces self-consistent multi-view outputs, which are used for baking high-definition texture maps.
From the paper
参考图注入与多视角一致性
论文称 Hunyuan3D-Paint 设计了一个保留细节的图像注入模块，目标是让参考图在语义和细节上都尽量对齐。论文还提到，多视角扩散通常通过在自注意力中加入 3D 感知的约束来保持视角间一致，并举例 Zero123++ 和 MVDiffusion 的做法，但这些是相关工作的描述，并非 Hunyuan3D-Paint 本身的具体实现。
2e0d0789-p019-c03 · PDF p. 19 · Original passage
Carefully adhering to these details can significantly improve the quality of the generated textures. Based on these advantages, Hunyuan3D-Paint is designed with a detailed preserving image injection module according to the philosophy of aligning the reference image not only semantically but also following the details as closely as possible. Multi-view Images Generation.Due to the viewpoint bias and multi-view inconsistency inherent in training-free image diffusion models, multi-view image diffusion was developed to alleviate these issues by utilizing large-scale 3D datasets, such as objaverse and objaverse-xl [20, 19]. Most works force the multi-view generated latents to communicate with each other by manipulating the self-attention layers with 3D-aware masks [ 36, 48, 88, 60, 95, 79, 78, 55]. For example, Zero123++ [78] first treats the multi-view attention as a self-attention on a large image, which is the spatial concatenation of six multi-view images. MVDiffusion [89] applies a correspondence-aware attention (CAA) to inform the model to focus only on the correlation among the spatially-close pixels.
From the paper
训练时的视角扰动
论文在训练细节中写道，参考图以随机方位角和 -20 到 20 度的固定俯仰范围渲染。作者认为这种变化会打破参考图与生成图之间的一致性，从而提高纹理生成框架的鲁棒性。
2e0d0789-p009-c04 · PDF p. 9 · Original passage
Multi-view images are rendered under the illumination of an even white light to accommodate our delighting model. Specifically, we render the reference image with a random azimuth and a fixed range of elevation from -20 to 20 degrees. This variation disrupts the consistency between the reference and generated images, thereby increasing the robustness of our texture generation framework. We directly train on 512 × 512 resolution with a total of 80,000 steps, a batch size of 48, and a learning rate of 5×10 −5. We use 1000 warm-up steps and the "trailing" scheduler proposed by ZSNR. General Text- and Image-to-Texture.It is worth noting that Hunyuan3D-Paint not only generates high-quality texture maps for generated meshes but also supports arbitrary texture generation guided by any text or image input provided by the user for any geometric model. 2e0d0789-p003-c04 · PDF p. 3 · Original passage
The comparison shows the superiority of Hunyuan3D 2.0 in alignment between conditional images and generated meshes, generation of fine-grained details, and human preference ratings. 2 Hunyuan3D 2.0 Architecture In this section, we elaborate on the model architecture of Hunyuan3D 2.0, focusing on two main components: the shape generation model and the texture generation model. Fig. 2 illustrates the pipeline of Hunyuan3D 2.0 for creating a high-resolution textured 3D asset. Given an input image, Hunyuan3D-DiT initially generates a high-fidelity bare mesh via the shape generation model. This model comprises a Hunyuan3D-ShapeV AE and a Hunyuan3D-DiT, which will be discussed in Sec. 3. Subsequently, by leveraging strong geometric priors and the input image, we introduce Hunyuan3D- Paint as our texture generation model in Sec. 4. This model produces self-consistent multi-view outputs, which are used for baking high-definition texture maps.

PaperLens inference
为什么形状阶段可能更脆弱（推断）
形状生成只能依赖输入图像，遮挡和杂乱背景可能让模型把背景误当成物体、或补全错误的被遮挡部分，这些几何错误会直接进入裸网格。第二阶段的法线图和位置图都来自这个网格，因此第一阶段的错误会沿流程传递，而纹理阶段很难修正几何上的错误。

PaperLens inference
纹理阶段的光照风险（推断，需谨慎）
光照变化更可能影响纹理：若参考图中的明暗和阴影被直接烘焙进贴图，AR 中的光照与之会不一致。论文的 Paint 流程包含「预处理与烘焙」等技术，图2中的标签还出现了 Image Delighting（去光照）模块，但这些是 PaperLens 对图中文字的理解，段落并未说明它如何处理光照或其效果。
2e0d0789-p003-c03 · PDF p. 3 · Original passage
Then, we build up a dual-single stream transformer [ 45] on the latent space of our V AE with the flow-matching [53, 24] objective. Our texture generation model – Hunyuan3D-Paint is made of a novel mesh-conditioned multi-view generation pipeline and a number of sophisticated techniques for preprocessing and baking multi-view images into high-resolution texture maps. We performed an in-depth comparison ofHunyuan3D 2.0 in relation to leading 3D generation models worldwide, including three commercial closed-source end-to-end products, an end-to-end opensourced model Trellis [101], and several separate models [9, 37, 99, 111, 55, 59] for shape and texture generation. We report visual and quantitative evaluation results across three dimensions: generated textured mesh, bare mesh, and texture map. We also provided user study results on 300 test cases involving 50 participants. The comparison shows the superiority of Hunyuan3D 2.0 in alignment between conditional images and generated meshes, generation of fine-grained details, and human preference ratings.

Paper: MDA: A Formal Approach to Game Design and Game Research Robin Hunicke, Marc LeBlanc, Robert Zubek
Reading goal: Get a general overview of this paper

Turn 1

For A · previous PaperLens reply
(first message of the conversation)
Learner message
好，那我想問一下，這篇論文裡面的「fun」到底是由什麼組成的？是不是就是遊戲的規則和目標？
Move the simulated learner was given
Ask what the paper says 'fun' is made of.
PaperLens reply (expand a source to see the cited text; highlighted = evidence quote that occurs in it)
Short answer
論文的檢索段落中並沒有明確把「fun」定義為一個獨立概念。MDA 的三個核心部分是 Mechanics、Dynamics 和 Aesthetics，其中 Aesthetics 指玩家與遊戲系統互動時產生的理想情緒反應，最接近你說的「fun」。所以「fun」不等於規則和目標本身：規則屬於 Mechanics，會透過 Dynamics 產生 Aesthetics 體驗。目前找到的段落不足以確認論文如何直接定義 fun。
f024c53f-p002-c00 · PDF p. 2 · Original passage
The difference between games and other entertainment products (such as books, music, movies and plays) is that their consumption is relatively unpredictable. The string of events that occur during gameplay and the outcome of those events are unknown at the time the product is finished. The MDA framework formalizes the consumption of games by breaking them into their distinct components: ...and establishing their design counterparts: Mechanics describes the particular components of the game, at the level of data representation and algorithms. Dynamics describes the run-time behavior of the mechanics acting on player inputs and each others’ outputs over time. Aesthetics describes the desirable emotional responses evoked in the player, when she interacts with the game system. Fundamental to this framework is the idea that games are more like artifacts than media. By this we mean that the content of a game is its behavior – not the media that streams out of it towards the player.
f024c53f-p002-c01 · PDF p. 2 · Original passage
Thinking about games as designed artifacts helps frame them as systems that build behavior via interaction. It supports clearer design choices and analysis at all levels of study and development. MDA in Detail MDA as Lens Each component of the MDA framework can be thought of as a “lens” or a “view” of the game – separate, but causally linked. [LeBlanc, 2004b]. From the designer’s perspective, the mechanics give rise to dynamic system behavior, which in turn leads to particular aesthetic experiences. From the player’s perspective, aesthetics set the tone, which is born out in observable dynamics and eventually, operable mechanics. When working with games, it is helpful to consider both the designer and player perspectives. It helps us observe how even small changes in one layer can cascade into others. In addition, thinking about the player encourages experience-driven (as opposed to feature-driven) design.
From the paper
三層之間的關係
論文說 Mechanics 是遊戲在資料表示與演算法層面的組成部分，Dynamics 是這些機制在執行時、面對玩家輸入與彼此輸出時的行為，Aesthetics 則是玩家與系統互動時被引發的理想情緒反應。從設計者角度看，機制產生動態系統行為，進而帶來特定的美學體驗。

Explanation
為什麼規則不等於 fun
可以把規則和目標理解為設計的起點，而 fun 更像玩家在遊玩過程中實際感受到的結果。論文的框架是從規則出發，經過系統運作，最後落在玩家的情緒體驗上，因此規則是必要條件之一，但不是 fun 本身。

Short answer
你的理解大致正確。Dynamics 指的是 Mechanics 在遊戲運行時的行為，它會隨時間作用於玩家的輸入，以及系統元件彼此的輸出，也就是遊戲跑起來後實際表現出來的互動過程。

The string of events that occur during gameplay and the outcome of those events are unknown at the time the product is finished. The MDA framework formalizes the consumption of games by breaking them into their distinct components: ...and establishing their design counterparts: Mechanics describes the particular components of the game, at the level of data representation and algorithms. From the paper
論文如何定義 Dynamics
論文指出 Dynamics 描述 Mechanics 在運行時的行為，作用於玩家輸入以及彼此的輸出，並且隨時間展開。Mechanics 是資料表示與演算法層面的組成，Dynamics 則是這些組成在執行時的表現。
f024c53f-p002-c00 · PDF p. 2 · Original passage
The difference between games and other entertainment products (such as books, music, movies and plays) is that their consumption is relatively unpredictable. The string of events that occur during gameplay and the outcome of those events are unknown at the time the product is finished. The MDA framework formalizes the consumption of games by breaking them into their distinct components: ...and establishing their design counterparts: Mechanics describes the particular components of the game, at the level of data representation and algorithms. Dynamics describes the run-time behavior of the mechanics acting on player inputs and each others’ outputs over time. Aesthetics describes the desirable emotional responses evoked in the player, when she interacts with the game system. Fundamental to this framework is the idea that games are more like artifacts than media. By this we mean that the content of a game is its behavior – not the media that streams out of it towards the player.
Explanation
用白話理解：規則本身與規則跑起來的過程
可以把 Mechanics 想成寫好的規則，例如「被擊中會扣血」。Dynamics 則是遊戲進行中，玩家攻擊、對手反應、血量逐漸下降後，整體形成的互動局面。前者是靜態設計，後者是執行中的過程。
No cited passage
Illustrative example · not from the paper
假設性例子：限時對戰遊戲
假設規則是「每局限時三分鐘，時間到就比分數」。玩家一開始可能穩健累積分數，但剩下三十秒時對手開始冒險搶分，雙方不斷互相回應。這種由時間限制與對手行為交錯而產生的緊張局面，就是 Dynamics 的運行情況（此例為說明用，並非論文內容）。
No cited passage
From the paper
論文中的相關說明：挑戰感從何而來
論文舉例說，挑戰感是由時間壓力和對手的遊戲行為等因素產生。這說明 Dynamics 如何支撐 Aesthetics 中的體驗，而不只是規則本身。
f024c53f-p003-c01 · PDF p. 3 · Original passage
It’s easy to see that supporting adversarial play and clear feedback about who is winning are essential to competitive games. If the player doesn’t see a clear winning condition, or feels like they can’t possibly win, the game is suddenly a lot less interesting. Dynamic Models Dynamics work to create aesthetic experiences. For example, challenge is created by things like time pressure and opponent play. Fellowship can be encouraged by sharing information across certain members of a session (a team) or supplying winning c onditions that are more difficult to achieve alone (such as capturing an enemy base). Expression comes from dynamics that encourage individual users to leave their mark: systems for purchasing, building or earning game items, for designing, constructing and changing le vels or worlds, and for creating personalized, unique characters . Dramatic tension comes from dynamics that en courage a rising tension, a release, and a denouement. As with aesthetics, we want our discussion of dynamics to remain as concrete as possible. By developing models that predict and describe gameplay dynamics, we can avoid some common design pitfalls.

  • none

For A · previous PaperLens reply
你的理解大致正確。Dynamics 指的是 Mechanics 在遊戲運行時的行為，它會隨時間作用於玩家的輸入，以及系統元件彼此的輸出，也就是遊戲跑起來後實際表現出來的互動過程。
  • [From the paper] 論文如何定義 Dynamics: 論文指出 Dynamics 描述 Mechanics 在運行時的行為，作用於玩家輸入以及彼此的輸出，並且隨時間展開。Mechanics 是資料表示與演算法層面的組成，Dynamics 則是這些組成在執行時的表現。
  • [Explanation] 用白話理解：規則本身與規則跑起來的過程: 可以把 Mechanics 想成寫好的規則，例如「被擊中會扣血」。Dynamics 則是遊戲進行中，玩家攻擊、對手反應、血量逐漸下降後，整體形成的互動局面。前者是靜態設計，後者是執行中的過程。
  • [Illustrative example · not from the paper] 假設性例子：限時對戰遊戲: 假設規則是「每局限時三分鐘，時間到就比分數」。玩家一開始可能穩健累積分數，但剩下三十秒時對手開始冒險搶分，雙方不斷互相回應。這種由時間限制與對手行為交錯而產生的緊張局面，就是 Dynamics 的運行情況（此例為說明用，並非論文內容）。
  • [From the paper] 論文中的相關說明：挑戰感從何而來: 論文舉例說，挑戰感是由時間壓力和對手的遊戲行為等因素產生。這說明 Dynamics 如何支撐 Aesthetics 中的體驗，而不只是規則本身。
Learner message
好，我現在比較懂 Dynamics 了。所以我的理解是：設計者是先想好 Mechanics，然後玩家也是照著這個順序一步一步體驗，先碰到機制，再有動態，最後才感受到 Aesthetics，跟設計者的順序一樣對吧？那這樣是不是代表玩家其實也是從規則開始理解遊戲的？
Move the simulated learner was given
State a misunderstanding: you think players experience mechanics first and aesthetics last, the same as designers.
PaperLens reply (expand a source to see the cited text; highlighted = evidence quote that occurs in it)
Short answer
這個理解只對了一半。論文描述的是兩個視角的方向相反：設計者從 Mechanics 出發，經 Dynamics 產生 Aesthetics；玩家則是從 Aesthetics 開始，再透過可觀察的 Dynamics 回到 Mechanics。所以不能說玩家也是照設計者的順序從規則開始理解遊戲。
f024c53f-p002-c01 · PDF p. 2 · Original passage
Thinking about games as designed artifacts helps frame them as systems that build behavior via interaction. It supports clearer design choices and analysis at all levels of study and development. MDA in Detail MDA as Lens Each component of the MDA framework can be thought of as a “lens” or a “view” of the game – separate, but causally linked. [LeBlanc, 2004b]. From the designer’s perspective, the mechanics give rise to dynamic system behavior, which in turn leads to particular aesthetic experiences. From the player’s perspective, aesthetics set the tone, which is born out in observable dynamics and eventually, operable mechanics. When working with games, it is helpful to consider both the designer and player perspectives. It helps us observe how even small changes in one layer can cascade into others. In addition, thinking about the player encourages experience-driven (as opposed to feature-driven) design.
From the paper
論文中的兩個方向
論文先從設計者的角度說明 Mechanics 產生動態系統行為，進而帶來特定的審美體驗；再從玩家的角度說明 Aesthetics 先設定基調，然後在可觀察的 Dynamics 中體現，最後才是可操作的 Mechanics。
f024c53f-p002-c01 · PDF p. 2 · Original passage
Thinking about games as designed artifacts helps frame them as systems that build behavior via interaction. It supports clearer design choices and analysis at all levels of study and development. MDA in Detail MDA as Lens Each component of the MDA framework can be thought of as a “lens” or a “view” of the game – separate, but causally linked. [LeBlanc, 2004b]. From the designer’s perspective, the mechanics give rise to dynamic system behavior, which in turn leads to particular aesthetic experiences. From the player’s perspective, aesthetics set the tone, which is born out in observable dynamics and eventually, operable mechanics. When working with games, it is helpful to consider both the designer and player perspectives. It helps us observe how even small changes in one layer can cascade into others. In addition, thinking about the player encourages experience-driven (as opposed to feature-driven) design.
Explanation
用白話說方向的差異
可以把它想成同一條鏈的兩個讀法：設計者是「規則 → 行為 → 感受」，玩家的感受先出現，然後才回頭去察覺行為與規則。兩者關注的層次相同，但起點和推進方向不同。
No cited passage
Limitation / uncertainty
這段論文沒有證明的部分
段落描述的是兩種視角的框架，並沒有研究真實玩家如何逐步學習或體驗遊戲，因此不能據此推斷玩家實際上是否先理解規則。若你想確認玩家的認知順序，需要其他研究或實證資料。

Learner message
那我想到上課的例子：老師說有一款遊戲機制很多、功能也很多，但玩起來還是很無聊。這跟論文的三層架構有關嗎？如果機制都有，為什麼還是不好玩？
Move the simulated learner was given
Ask how this connects to a lecture example where a game was boring even though it had many features.
PaperLens reply (expand a source to see the cited text; highlighted = evidence quote that occurs in it)
Short answer
論文的架構支持「機制多不代表好玩」這個看法：機制先產生 Dynamics（執行時的動態行為），再引出 Aesthetics（玩家的情緒體驗），中間任何一層沒有接好，都可能讓玩家覺得無聊。論文也明說沒有公式能決定哪些元素組合會產生「fun」。不過，段落中沒有直接討論老師舉的「機制很多但無聊」案例。
f024c53f-p002-c01 · PDF p. 2 · Original passage
Thinking about games as designed artifacts helps frame them as systems that build behavior via interaction. It supports clearer design choices and analysis at all levels of study and development. MDA in Detail MDA as Lens Each component of the MDA framework can be thought of as a “lens” or a “view” of the game – separate, but causally linked. [LeBlanc, 2004b]. From the designer’s perspective, the mechanics give rise to dynamic system behavior, which in turn leads to particular aesthetic experiences. From the player’s perspective, aesthetics set the tone, which is born out in observable dynamics and eventually, operable mechanics. When working with games, it is helpful to consider both the designer and player perspectives. It helps us observe how even small changes in one layer can cascade into others. In addition, thinking about the player encourages experience-driven (as opposed to feature-driven) design.
f024c53f-p002-c03 · PDF p. 2 · Original passage
Here we see that each game pursues multiple aesthetic goals, in varying degrees. Charades emphasizes Fellowship over Challenge; Quake provides Challenge as a main element of gameplay. And while there is no Grand Unified Theory of games or formula that details the combination and proportion of elements that will result in “fun”, this M D A Designer Player The designer and player each have a different perspective. 1. Sensation Game as sense-pleasure 2. Fantasy Game as make-believe 3. Narrative Game as drama 4. Challenge Game as obstacle course 5. Fellowship Game as social framework 6. Discovery Game as uncharted territory 7. Expression Game as self-discovery 8. Submission Game as pastime Rules “Fun” System Mechanics Aesthetics Dynamics
From the paper
三層之間的因果鏈
論文說，從設計者的角度看，機制會產生動態系統行為，進而帶來特定的美學體驗。論文也強調各層之間是因果相連的，小的改變可能沿著層次一路影響到其他層。
f024c53f-p002-c01 · PDF p. 2 · Original passage
Thinking about games as designed artifacts helps frame them as systems that build behavior via interaction. It supports clearer design choices and analysis at all levels of study and development. MDA in Detail MDA as Lens Each component of the MDA framework can be thought of as a “lens” or a “view” of the game – separate, but causally linked. [LeBlanc, 2004b]. From the designer’s perspective, the mechanics give rise to dynamic system behavior, which in turn leads to particular aesthetic experiences. From the player’s perspective, aesthetics set the tone, which is born out in observable dynamics and eventually, operable mechanics. In addition, thinking about the player encourages experience-driven (as opposed to feature-driven) design.
PaperLens inference
為什麼機制多不等於好玩
依照這個因果鏈，機制數量只是起點；如果它們在執行時產生的動態沒有支持玩家想要的情緒體驗，就可能出現老師說的情況。這是我根據論文架構做的推論，論文本身沒有討論「無聊」。
No cited passage
From the paper
論文對「fun」的態度
論文指出每款遊戲會追求多種美學目標，程度不一，並且承認沒有能決定哪些元素組合會產生「fun」的公式。這表示論文並不提供一套保證有趣的配方。

Illustrative example · not from the paper
一個假設性的例子
假設一款遊戲有很多武器與技能（機制），但戰鬥節奏總是一樣，玩家很快就能預測結果（動態單調），因此很難產生挑戰或驚喜的感受（美學）。這只是為了說明概念，不是論文中的例子。

論文提到的兩款遊戲

Explanation
如何拿來和上課內容對照
你可以把這兩款遊戲當作「美學目標不同」的比較點，再去對照老師上課講的內容，看老師是在談哪一層的問題。例如，若老師講的是規則設計，可以對應到 Mechanics；若講的是玩起來的節奏或回饋，可以對應到 Dynamics；若講的是玩家的情緒感受，則對應到 Aesthetics。
No cited passage
Illustrative example · not from the paper
如果要自己做三層案例
假設你選一款熟悉的遊戲，可以先列出它的規則與數值（Mechanics），再描述這些規則在遊戲中運作時產生的節奏或互動（Dynamics），最後寫下玩家可能感受到的情緒，例如緊張或放鬆（Aesthetics）。這是示範用的做法，不是論文中的案例。
